[abstract]
== Abstract

AEGIS — the *ARGUS Ergonomics & Graphical Interface Standard* — is the design language of the ARGUS surface: the visual grammar, the interface philosophy, and the behavioural canon everything on the console obeys. The name is deliberate: an aegis is a protective canon, and everything on the surface is built *under the AEGIS*. Where link:../../../docs/workspace-model.adoc[the workspace model] rules on where logic lives in the mise stack, AEGIS rules on what the surface *is* — what a pane may show, where its machinery hides, what links panes together, and what LCARS means when it is taken seriously. This is a *living document*, updated as the design conversation progresses.

== Status

[cols="1,3"]
|===
| 2026-10-31 (DOMAINS is OPENSHIFT; the controller answers `cluster`) | docs/stratus.adoc D16, grilled with the operator: a foundation is built first and an application built into it, so OpenShift, a VM and ChRIS are layers and a Trusted Domain is the recipe of OpenShift with ChRIS on it, not a machine kind. The bare cluster's verbs are `cluster` (`cluster new`, `cluster build`, `cluster status`…), and the stratus dashboard's first tile is OPENSHIFT, counting CLUSTERS where it counted TRUSTED DOMAINS. `cluster` and `cluster <name>` take the screen as the controller, as `domain` did; the controller's pills, empty view and log speak of clusters; the prompt's segment key is `cluster`. The Trusted Domain mark and splash stay: they brand the cloud session, whose purpose is Trusted Domains. Folder `clusters/` under the tenancy, the tag `mise-cluster`.
| 2026-10-31 (a document reads as its page) | The operator: an opened `.md` or `.adoc` showed as plain bytes everywhere, the Viewer pane included, and AsciiDoc had no colours even in the editor. Now a file opened to read in the browser shows its source in the editor's colours (any language the editor knows, past 512 KB plain), and a Markdown or AsciiDoc document carries RENDERED on its header for the page it describes, SOURCE to return; a Viewer pane beside the browser shows such a document rendered. AsciiDoc gains a line-oriented syntax mode, shared by the editor and the read view (`features/edit/syntax.ts` holds the one colour table both use). The engines (marked, Asciidoctor in SECURE mode, DOMPurify over both) load only at the first render. A rendered document stays inside its pane: no script or style, its anchors renamed so none meets the page's ids, an in-document link scrolls the pane, a link off the page opens a new tab, a link to a neighbouring file goes nowhere, an image is named by its alt text rather than fetched. Proven by unit tests of the renderer and the colours, and smoke `files-document` (both kinds, both views, the Viewer pane). Law standing: a-file-reads-as-what-it-is. | `features/view/document.ts`, `documentEngines.ts`, `panel.ts`; `features/edit/syntax.ts`, `asciidoc.ts`, `field.ts`; `features/files/panel.ts`; `lcars/argus.css`
| 2026-10-29 (the cloud door asks READ or MANAGE) | docs/stratus.adoc D10 and D14, argued with the operator: whether a stratus session may act on the tenancy is fixed when porter starts it, and it was asked for with `stratus oci --spend`, a flag no control on the surface could reach and a word nobody would know to type, which broke the rule that what the console does the dashboard can do. Now bare `stratus oci` asks at the door, as trek asks its settings before the first turn: a sheet with READ (look only; nothing outside this host changes), MANAGE (act as well, each act after its own lock and the name typed back) and NOT NOW, answered by a press, the answer's first letter, or Escape. `stratus oci read` and `stratus oci manage` answer in words; `--spend` and `--read` are refused by name. The ChRIS dashboard gains a CLOUD tile where the page came through a porter, after the UNIVERSE; it runs the console's own line, so the question is one question pressed or typed. The tier itself stays fixed for the session: changing the answer is entering again. Proven by unit tests of the verb, the sheet and the tile. | `app/cloudDoor.ts`; `console/argusLang.ts`; `compositions/chris/tiles.ts`; `features/launcher/about.ts`; `app/main.ts`
| 2026-10-26 (CAPACITY opens a pane; SIZE asks on open) | #1284, found live on pangea by the operator: pressing the CAPACITY tile, or a row on it, sent `capacity` as a console line and the answer printed as text, so the press read as dead; and the controller's SIZE sat on "reading what a domain is sized against" because NEW opened on SIZE without asking for its sizing. The tile and its rows now open the CAPACITY pane (`features/capacity/panel.ts`, `app/capacityPane.ts`): the `stratus.capacity` model as a listing under the façade, one block per family, a row per limit and zone, FAMILY · LIMIT · ZONE (the expanse) · USED · FREE · OF, the figures red where nothing is left, the bar naming the region and the families; the typed `capacity pane [<family>]` opens the same, and bare `capacity` stays the session's text. The tile's rows read NOT READ, not NO FAMILY, when the session did not answer, since `capacity` now falls back to every family the region offers when no spec names one. `domain_choose` on NEW asks `domain size` as a press on SIZE does. Proven by the pane's rows test, the tile test, the controller screen test and stratus's capacity test. | `features/capacity/panel.ts`; `app/capacityPane.ts`, `main.ts`, `panes.ts`; `console/argusLang.ts`; `compositions/stratus/tiles.ts`; `features/controller/screen.ts`; `lcars/argus.css`, `index.html`; `packages/stratus/src/commands/capacity.ts`
| 2026-10-26 (DOMAINS lists the tenancy) | #1286, docs/stratus.adoc D15. The operator's first stratus session on pangea's porter read DOMAINS · NONE while the tenancy held harbor, serving and billing: the session listed only its own folder. `domain status` now lists every domain the tenancy holds by its `mise-domain` tag, so the tile and the controller show them; one this host keeps nothing of wears the class FOUND · NOT ADOPTED, its row reads so in place of a last step, its OVERVIEW note says what it is and carries ADOPT, which sends `domain adopt <name>`; PLAN, VERIFY, BUILD and TEAR DOWN read REFUSED on it with "adopt <name> first". | 2026-10-27 (a paint does not wait on the region) | Found live on pangea by the operator in a stratus session: the console attached, printed its banner and offered no prompt for minutes, and the dashboard stayed empty. The dashboard asks `capacity` at paint, which with no family named walks every family the region offers, limit by limit and zone by zone, one read at a time against a throttled API; stratus declared no holds, so that read held the whole session and the console's greeting (`motd`) waited behind it in the lane. Now `capacity` and `spend` hold nothing and run beside any line; `capacity` keeps what it read for ten minutes and reads four limits at once; and a paint waits one and a half seconds for `capacity`, paints DOMAINS with CAPACITY · READING when it has not answered, and is painted again when it lands, one `capacity` on its way at a time. Proven by the tile test (a slow capacity, one ask, the repaint), stratus's capacity test (the second read asks the cloud nothing) and the backend test (the holds). | `compositions/stratus/tiles.ts`; `app/main.ts`; `packages/stratus/src/commands/{capacity,domain}.ts`, `backend.ts`
| 2026-10-26 (a takeover keeps the frame) | #1285, found live on pangea by the operator in a stratus session: the TD controller's gutter was a hundred pixels wider than ARGUS's, its head and body elbows met flush with no gap, its body opened on bare text under no strip, and its readouts wore trek's column heads; trek's bridge, which the controller was built from, had the same geometry. Both drew a frame of their own. Now one frame serves every screen that takes over: `features/takeover/frame.ts` builds the page's own skeleton — the gutter at `--lfw` with a brand block and a seat at its head, the banner, a face and the band beside them, the gap, the views as the page's seats (`panel-3` on, LEAVE closing the column as `panel-7`, the chosen one lit as LANG is when live), and beside them the body's elbow as the page draws it with the console closed: the band at the head of the column, flush with the first seat, its inner fillet under the band's left end, both in the colour of the seat they join (lit with it when that view is chosen), and the page's own `main` below holding the body and the log as panes, each under its titled strip. Its measurements are argus.css's: each `takeover-*` class is listed beside the page's own (`.left-frame-top`, `.right-frame-top`, `.wrap`, `.left-frame`, `.right-frame`), so a change to the frame moves every screen drawn in it. The bridge and the controller keep only their content. Proven by the two screens' unit tests, the smoke stages `trek-bridge` and `td-controller`, and the theme canon (zero differences on the page). Law: a-takeover-keeps-the-frame. | `features/takeover/frame.ts`, `lcars/takeover.css`, `lcars/argus.css`; `features/controller/screen.ts`, `controller.css`; `features/trek/bridge.ts`, `trek.css`
| 2026-10-25 (a pull runs as a joblet; STOP is kill %N) | Epic #1273, slice 5, grilled with the operator (question 9: "this means that long running tasks don't hog the queue?"). PACS PULL (a study, a series, PULL N SHOWN) and the cohort's pulls (all, a row) send `pull … &`: the line the operator could type, echoed as it is sent, run as a joblet, so the console and every other press stay the operator's while a retrieve goes on. The badges follow it as before: a joblet relays its progress to the surface it came from, and hands that surface any model it answered with (the cohort its pull brought home), which the ambient routes already take. Each running joblet is a row on the LANE instrument, `%1 pull … · RUNNING` (or `WAITING · FG %1 TO ANSWER`, amber), with a STOP that types `kill %1` at the console; the console prints `[1] … &` when one starts and `[1]+ Done`, `Failed (exit N): …`, `Stopped` or the waiting line when it ends or asks. The RESTART question names each joblet it would end (its log kept), and a porter login that finds the daemon on older code keeps it, rather than restart it, while joblets run. Presses whose next step reads the answer, or that ask a question in front, stay in front: CREATE FEED and PROCESS (the cohort adopts the feed they answer), catalogue RUN (it reads the scheduled feed), feed DELETE (it confirms), DAG REFRESH (index movement, already off the lane), and the TD controller's build and teardown (they follow their steps aside already, and the screen is built on following them). Proved live on a private daemon: `du ~ &` ran as `%1` with its LANE row and STOP, `nosuchcommand &` printed `[2]+ Failed (exit 1): chell: command not found: nosuchcommand`, and STOP printed `[1]+ Stopped  du ~` and took the row away. | `app/laneInstrument.ts`, `app/restart.ts`, `app/main.ts`, `features/pacs/panel.ts`, `features/gather/panel.ts`, `lcars/argus.css`, brasa `core/joblets.ts`, calypso `daemon/server.ts` (`/joblets`), porter `versions.ts`, `app.ts`, menu `messages.ts`
| 2026-10-23 (the DOMAINS tile is the controller's door) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D13. Pressing the DOMAINS tile opened home (the files listing of domains), so on a tablet the controller could only be reached by typing `domain`. The tile now sends `domain`, opening the TD controller on the domain it chooses, with the others as pills; its verb reads OPEN THE CONTROLLER, and a row still opens its own domain. Home stays on FILES. Alongside it, an adopted domain shows the ChRIS steps its checkout did: harbor read 11 of 15 and UP because adoption carried only the machine's steps; adoption now also carries `postinstall` and, from the checkout's evidence, `chris-deploy`, `chris-seed` and `smoke`, and `domain adopt` on an adopted domain catches up (harbor now reads 15 of 15, SERVING). Proven by the tile and adoption unit tests and live on harbor. | `compositions/stratus/tiles.ts`; `features/launcher/about.ts`; `packages/stratus/src/domain/adopt.ts`, `commands/build.ts`
| 2026-10-23 (the tiles answer while a build is followed) | Epic #1056, docs/calypso.adoc (the session daemon). Live, quay's build was followed by a play for an hour, and a second ARGUS opened on the session drew an empty DASHBOARD: every tile's read waited on the lane behind the follow. A follow now steps out of the lane between the task's lines (`aside`, as a question already did), so tiles, the controller's reads and other lines run meanwhile. The same session showed two more faults, fixed with it: `domain quay` typed in a browser whose tiles had not claimed the name opened the controller in another browser (a browser's own line is now its own or the session's), and the play left behind by a closed browser could not be stopped from the one still open (a line whose surface has gone is anyone's to stop). Proven by calypso's stop and surface-line tests, brasa's runner and turn tests, and live on quay. | `packages/menu/src/surface.ts`; `packages/brasa/src/core/surface.ts`, `runner.ts`, `turn.ts`, `engine.ts`; `packages/calypso/src/daemon/server.ts`, `launch.ts`; `packages/stratus/src/commands/build.ts`
| 2026-10-23 (BUILD builds as far as the steps go, ChRIS included) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D14. The build's steps now run through ChRIS (`postinstall`, `verify`, `chris-deploy`, `chris-seed`, `smoke`), so BUILD sends `domain build <name>` with no `--through`, and `tenant.mise` claims the smoke step done. The grid and the CHRIS view show those steps as any other, with their evidence; a domain whose smoke passed reads SERVING. SMOKE AGAIN reads SMOKE AGAIN · LATER: the build runs the journey once, and running it again is a line not written yet. Proven by the model and screen unit tests and a fake build through `smoke`. | `features/controller/model.ts`, `screen.ts`; `packages/stratus/manifests/tenant.mise`
| 2026-10-23 (a build left part way reads STOPPED) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D13. A domain with steps done, none running and not up was SIZING · NOTHING BUILT, as if nothing had run: quay answered NO at the billing question with five steps done and read so. It now reads STOPPED, drawn in SIZING's honey; the line under it says PLANNED · NOTHING BILLED when the next step is the first that bills, NOT FINISHED otherwise (BILLING · NOT FINISHED once money runs). SIZING means a spec and nothing run. Proven by unit tests for `condition_derive` and `conditionWhy_derive` and the fake build's last state event. | `packages/menu/src/substrate.ts`; `packages/stratus/src/domain/readout.ts`; `features/controller/model.ts`, `controller.css`
| 2026-10-23 (SIZE chooses the zone a new domain's names live under) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D14. The `stratus.domain.size` model gains `dnsZones`, the tenancy's DNS zones, and its `baseDomain` falls back to the only one. SIZE draws a ZONE row of pills beside FAMILY with the chosen zone lit; ADDRESS reads `<name>.<zone>`, or NO ZONE · CHOOSE ONE ON SIZE. COMMIT reads COMMIT · NO ZONE and stays dark until a zone is chosen, and its line names `--base-domain` only when the zone is not the session's default. Found live: COMMIT had sent `domain new quay` into a session with no zone. Also found live: the DOMAINS tile's paint now claims each domain's name for the language, so `domain <name>` typed in a fresh session opens the controller instead of running as text.
| 2026-10-23 (COMMIT writes the spec, then plays the tenant) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D14. SIZE's COMMIT shows and sends two lines: `domain new <name> …`, then `play ~/tenant.mise --param NAME=<name>`, the manifest stratus ships in the session's home. The play is the controller's own line, so the build's billing question stands on its strip and its state events redraw BUILD, where COMMIT takes the screen once the spec is written; a refused spec leaves SIZE as it was, and a NO at billing leaves the spec written and nothing made.
| 2026-10-23 (`domain` takes the screen as the TD controller) | Epic #1056, docs/stratus.adoc D13 and D14, built whole against the mockup v2. In a stratus session `domain` (or `domain <name>`, a DOMAINS row, a domain's pill) opens the TD controller over ARGUS, trek's layout under the theme's palette: the upper gutter carries the STRATUS block and a pill per domain with + NEW; the header CONDITION (SIZING, ASKING, BUILDING, UP, SERVING, FAILED, GONE) with the billing line under it, the steps done, the class badge (BUILT HERE, ADOPTED · PLANS ONLY, CHECKOUT · READ ONLY) and the lock, then DOMAIN, COST, BUILD and SINCE LAST COMMAND; the lower gutter the views OVERVIEW, SIZE, BUILD, CLUSTER, CHRIS and TEARDOWN, and LEAVE. Every control sends a session line, shown in the domain log as typed (a domain's pill sends `domain <name>`; PLAN, VERIFY, SPEND; BUILD sends `domain build <name> --through run`; KILL stops following, then sends `kill <name>-build`; LIFT THE LOCK sends `domain protect <name> off`, a line of its own; TEAR DOWN sends `domain teardown <name>`), and a prompt at the foot takes any line. The engine's questions stand on a strip over the log: the billing question answered by YES · BILL or NO, never skipped; teardown's by the name typed back, DESTROY lit only when it matches. SIZE is the levers over what `domain size` read once: fit per zone and cost per hour redraw as they move, as presentation; COMMIT, held until the name is a free DNS label, sends `domain new` with what differs from the starting spec. Orange means money runs, red destroying. The controller reads `domain status` when it opens and after each line, and takes the `stratus.domain.state` events a build shows between; the tab's title carries the condition (`ASKING · quay`); a step finishing, the engine asking and a failure are heard. A build is not a game: LEAVE and Esc never stop one, and opening again finds it. Under 1250 px the controller does not take the screen, says so, and the line's answer prints as text. CLUSTER's nodes and projects and CHRIS's SMOKE AGAIN and ATTACH wait on the cluster in the tree, and say so. Proven by unit tests for the model and the screen, the smoke stage td-controller (desk and tablet sizes) over `stratus --fake`, and a fake build driven through the controller from SIZING to the billing question, YES · BILL, BUILDING with the running step, and UP. | `features/controller/model.ts`, `screen.ts`, `controller.css`; `app/tdController.ts`; `compositions/stratus/lang.ts`, `tiles.ts`; `app/main.ts`; `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs`
| 2026-10-22 (one login, a switch to the cloud) | stratus.adoc D14. The ChRIS login is the only login: a page that came through a porter switches to the same person's stratus session with `stratus oci` and back with `chris`, verbs of the frame (they act on the door, never on the session). The porter starts or rejoins the stratus session on the credentials filed under the person's ChRIS identity and moves its cookie; the page follows to the greeter while it boots, or straight to the mount. A session that may spend is asked for in words: `stratus oci --spend` asks for `spend` typed, and is otherwise read-only. A page that came in any other way refuses the verbs by name.
| 2026-10-22 (a pipeline's run lights its feed as a plugin's does) | Issue #1208, slice 2. A pipeline run answers a model of its own, `pipeline.scheduled` (every node it made, with each node's output folder and how it settled, and the nodes nothing in it runs off), instead of passing as one plugin's `run.scheduled` with the first node's id and an empty output path. The catalogue's RUN lights the feed the work landed in for either kind. `run.scheduled` gains `status`, CUBE's word as the run settled or `detached`. A variable is a session's, not a surface's: `set` answers a `variables.list` model and `set A` answers A's own model, which a variables pane can draw later; ARGUS draws none yet, and its console runs `A=$(…)`, `set` and `unset` as chell does.
| 2026-10-22 (DOMAINS says what each domain costs an hour) | Epic #1056, step 4c. The `stratus.domains` model gains an optional `cost` (an hour and so far, at list price, with its currency), filled by `domain status` from `spend`'s pricing of the domain's compartment. DOMAINS shows each priced domain's rate on its row, before the cluster's name so a narrow tile cuts the name and not the money (`harbor  09-run  $0.62/h  harbor.oci…`) and the domains' total among its figures (`$1.24/H`); a domain the daemon could not price, or an older daemon, shows none rather than a zero. The tile's words now say "and what it costs per hour", the extension the operator approved with them. Proven by the unit test and desk and phone screenshots over the live tenancy. | `compositions/stratus/tiles.ts`, `features/launcher/about.ts`
| 2026-10-22 (the HOST lamp names what each tier hands the daemon) | Epic #1056, step 4a. Calypso gains a fourth host-control tier, `tasks`: the engine may start long jobs that change a cloud and bill (docs/stratus.adoc, D10), named on its own and never granted by a bare `--host-control`. The amber HOST lamp already read the attach ack's tiers, but its title said `!` and pipes run on the daemon's host whatever the tiers were, which is false for a daemon granted `tasks` alone. The title now names each tier for what it hands the daemon, in the ack's order (`jobs may change the cloud and bill`); the lamp's text, `HOST <tiers>`, and law `host-control-is-annunciated` are unchanged. Chell's attach banner says the same per tier. Proven by a unit test and over a private `stratus --fake --host-control tasks` daemon. | `app/status.ts`
| 2026-10-21 (a stratus dashboard leads with its domains) | Epic #1056, step 3. A session over `stratus` has tiles of its own (docs/stratus.adoc, D11), asked of the session at paint time from the commands a terminal runs: DOMAINS leads in the wide seat with each Trusted Domain from `domain status` (its last finished build step and its cluster; a domain with a step gone wrong, such as an expired ISO link, in red, with a NEEDS A LOOK figure), a row opening the domain's folder; CAPACITY follows FILES with `capacity` (per zone, free of permitted, for the shape families the domains' specs name; nothing free in red; with no family named it teaches the line `capacity E5`). FILES says what a stratus home holds. Both read the wire's new models (`stratus.domains`, `stratus.capacity`) rather than the rendered text. The two tiles' descriptions are drafts for the operator's approval. Hourly cost on DOMAINS waits on how `spend` is run. Proven by the unit test, the smoke stage stratus-tiles (and frame-alone, which now counts the frame's five tiles among a composition's own) over `stratus --fake` and `calypso --backend null`, and desk and phone screenshots over the live tenancy. | `compositions/stratus/tiles.ts`, `features/launcher/about.ts`, `app/main.ts`, `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs`
| 2026-10-21 (a frame-alone session boots inside the frame) | Epic #1056, follow-up 2. Over `stratus` (and `null`) ARGUS booted with the dashboard alone on the screen: header, gutter and console zoomed away, so the identity and the prompt the session exists for were off stage until Esc. The ChRIS landing law (2026-09-17, a session begins at the dashboard) zooms only on a browser's first visit, and a frame-alone daemon serves on a fresh port each start, so its browser storage is always a first visit and every boot hid the frame. A frame-alone session now boots with the dashboard on stage inside the whole frame: the header with its brand mark (the TD mark under stratus), the console drawer open at its prompt, the gutter. The DASHBOARD gutter still zooms it alone; a ChRIS session is unchanged. Proven over a private `stratus --fake` daemon by the smoke's boot check and the frame-alone stage, and by desk and phone screenshots. | `app/main.ts`, `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs`
| 2026-10-21 (a session over stratus wears the Trusted Domain's brand) | Epic #1056, slice 1b. ARGUS asks the daemon its backend and draws ChRIS's composition only for `chris`; a session over `stratus` now has a composition of its own, brand only in this slice: the Trusted Domain's North Star in the driver's ring (OCI: Oracle red) as the header's mark and the tab's, the same mark as the console's splash, and the credit line "a Trusted Domain console". What the composition owns in the page's markup carries `data-composition="stratus"` and stands only under it, as ChRIS's does. The tiles (DOMAINS, CAPACITY, TENANCY, STEPS), the domain pane and the controller are later slices of docs/stratus.adoc. | `index.html`, `app/main.ts`, `lcars/argus.css`, `assets/brand/td-oci.svg`; the mark from `@fnndsc/menu/logo/td`
| 2026-10-21 (the prompt bar draws a backend's segments) | Epic #1056, slice 1b. The wire's prompt context gains backend-neutral segments (docs/stratus.adoc, D9): a key, a label, a value and a tone each, filled by the backend in display order. The console's bar draws them in that order, a glyph by key (cloud, tenancy, region, user, domain, working directory) and a colour by tone, with its own PHYSICAL, duration and status telemetry trailing; a long identity, an e-mail address at a tenancy, is cut from the left at a fixed width and carries its whole value as a title. A ChRIS daemon sends no segments and its bar is drawn from the named fields exactly as before. The bar is its own pure module, `console/promptBar.ts`, tested by value; the glyphs and palette stay in `terminal.ts`, where `lint:prompt` holds them to chell's. | `console/promptBar.ts`, `console/terminal.ts`, `scripts/prompt-parity.mjs`
| 2026-10-19 (the moving bridge is proven) | Epic #1172, slice S7. The `trek-motion` smoke scenario, on both lanes, on seed 13 (a Klingon in the start quadrant): the standing order fills the long-range scan at the first prompt with its lines folded in the log, the Klingon wears the same mark on the scan, the tactical wheel and its target pill, a torpedo fired at it flies the printed track and the frames the picture played are counted against the log's lines kind by kind (torpedo points, kills, novas, moves, escapes, hits: every frame a line, the track's frames the printed points, the kill's burst after the last point, the scan agreeing with the state once the picture settles), and a view pill pressed during a quadrant crossing lands the ship in the new quadrant at once with the log whole. Two laws land with it: a mark means one thing everywhere (a lint that the scan, the wheels, the target pills and the legend all draw from the one symbol table and that no sector is drawn as its glyph) and a frame replays a printed line. | #1172; apps/argus/tests/smoke/smoke.mjs; scripts/aegis-lint.mjs
| 2026-10-19 (the bridge fits its window) | Epic #1172 follow-up. The stars breathed off their cells: a `<use>` placed by x,y cannot be scaled in place, every browser scales it about the drawing's corner, so a far star slid most of a cell toward 0,0 and back. Every mark is now a group that carries its place with the symbol drawn about the group's origin, and whatever scales or turns a mark (breathing, a heading, the slide) acts about the mark itself. The header keeps its values at a narrow window: three groups share the width, what moved since the last command sits under the mission, and the condition block and label pills are narrower. The left column is the long-range scan and the chart, the chart sized to the room left (engineering has its own view); a slider's ticks thin to every other and then to the ends when the track is narrow; the bridge's own lines in the log carry the prompt they answered (`Command: m`, `Course: 236`) and are set apart from the engine's. | #1172; apps/argus/src/features/trek/marks.ts; apps/argus/src/features/trek/trek.css
| 2026-10-20 (a question shows where it is answered) | Epic #1172 follow-up, from the operator's tablet. A station column shorter than its stations held a question's lit control below the fold: the torpedo's burst angles waited unseen, and the operator read it as no way to fire. A question opened now scrolls its lit control into view. Nothing about DICOM was broken. argus loads its image engines on demand as chunks whose names carry the build's hash; a tab loaded before a deploy holds the old names, and the server now has only the new ones. Any deploy does this to an open page — an `npm install -g` upgrade on titan, a `make cook` on pangea — and the page said nothing: an image pane simply never opened. Under honest-wait (a failure is visible and truthful), the page now watches for it (`app/stalePage.ts`): Vite's loader raises `vite:preloadError` for a failed chunk, and a native `unhandledrejection` carrying the browsers' words for a failed module load is the fallback. Once — however many chunks fail — the console says `argus: the server has a newer argus than this page — reload to continue`, and a notice stands over the stage, `ARGUS WAS UPDATED ON THE SERVER — THIS PAGE IS OLDER`, carrying the control that answers it, RELOAD. Proven in Chrome by a real failed module load, and by the smoke `stale-page`. |

Three changes. (1) A settle too big for one frame runs in slices of about 12 ms, one per frame, and says how far it has come (`SceneHandlers.progress`); the universe shows `SETTLING 3,034 SPHERES · 43%` on a bar over the field (`WaitProgress`, `features/wait/progress.ts`) while the space that stands stays drawn and live. Before the answer arrives the field says `ASKING THE SESSION FOR THE SPACE` and the title `UNIVERSE — ASKING THE SESSION …`. (2) A settle has a mode (`SettleMode`): `full` re-settles every node (a physics knob, a scale, a view); `new` holds what already stands and settles only newcomers; `hold` moves nothing when every node stands (a census toggle, a palette change). The universe paints `new` by default, so a space re-shown with remembered positions — a fresh login, a reload, the climb back out of a feed (which was a hidden eight-second settle) — appears at once, and a landing during a sweep settles only its own molecule instead of three thousand spheres a second. (3) A first paint no longer settles twice: a changed reach is set without settling the old graph (`physics_set(terms, false)`). The cost of a slice is the nodes that move, so a descent — a handful free among thousands pinned — still lands in one call for the flight that follows. Measured after: first open, ASKING at once, then the bar from 0 % to 99 % over 14 s with the page drawing throughout (worst frame 0.88 s, once); a reload with remembered positions, the space in 0.64 s. | 2026-09-26 (placed once, drawn everywhere) | ORRERY slice 10 (#722), as the operator chose it: the universe laid out once per identity, not once per browser. | 2026-09-30 (a node sits on its tubes) | The operator, in a graph's 2D projection: the tubes ran across the node circles, ending inside them. 2D draws nodes as discs in the plane the tubes are centred on, so a tube's near face stood in front of the disc; and a first fix by render order did nothing, because the tubes are transparent (their shader fades and pulses) and three draws every transparent thing after every opaque one whatever its order. The disc is now transparent at full opacity, ordered after the tubes, without the depth test: it covers the tube ends as a sphere's near hemisphere does in 3D. Verified by rendering the same feed before and after on a private daemon. Law: a-node-sits-on-its-tubes.

| 2026-10-03 (ARGUS and the calypso daemon say when they are different builds) | The operator, on pangea: "I clicked on a file in ~/me.txt -- the frame opened and showed EDIT -- I clicked that and NOTHING happened." Nothing in EDIT was broken: the operator's calypso daemon had run since 2026-10-01 while ARGUS was rebuilt beside it. The daemon serves ARGUS from disk on every load but runs the code it started with, so a freshly loaded ARGUS asked an old daemon for something it did not know, and nothing said why. Under honest-wait, ARGUS's build now writes its stamp (`build.json`: commit and minute) into the bundle; the calypso daemon reads it once when it starts and reports it in its attach ack (`stack.surface`); ARGUS compares its own stamp and, when they differ, says which is older and what to restart — on the status strip (`CALYPSO DAEMON OUT OF DATE` / `ARGUS OUT OF DATE`), in a dismissible notice over the stage, and on the console beneath the greeting with the two builds as evidence: `ARGUS is newer than the calypso daemon, so some controls won't work. Restart the calypso daemon.` or `ARGUS is older than the calypso daemon. Refresh to load the new ARGUS.` (Refresh is the control). The words name the two processes, never "session" or "page" — the operator's correction of the first draft. A daemon too old to report a stamp counts as older. A remote chell says the same at attach when its build and the daemon's differ. The boot's greeting moved to `app/greeting.ts` so its notes can follow it, and the host's ceilings ratcheted down (2,913 lines; `surface_start` 1,941). | `app/buildMatch.ts`, `app/greeting.ts`, `app/status.ts`, `app/main.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, calypso `daemon/static.ts`, chell `remote/buildMatch.ts`

| 2026-10-03 (the calypso daemon knows when it is stale) | #863, slice 1, grilled with the operator after #858's warning cried wolf: an ARGUS-only release (0.21.1) changed no daemon code, yet every running daemon read CALYPSO DAEMON OUT OF DATE, because the rule compared ARGUS's stamp alone. The one that can answer "is this process still running the code on its disk?" is the daemon, so it answers. It fingerprints its own packages when it starts (each one's version and the content of its compiled `dist/*.js`; content, not file times, since a reinstall of the same version rewrites every time without changing a byte, while a checkout rebuilt at the same commit changes bytes without changing a version). It reads the fingerprint again on every attach and once a minute, says `stale` in the attach ack, and pushes `stale` to every surface when the answer flips. ARGUS believes it: stale reads CALYPSO DAEMON OUT OF DATE, and the sentence says what happened ("The calypso daemon has been updated and should be restarted; some controls may not work until then. Restart the calypso daemon." — the operator's wording, replacing "ARGUS is newer than…", which no longer described it); not stale but a newer ARGUS is silent; an older ARGUS still reads Refresh. A daemon too old to say falls back to the stamp. An open ARGUS learns of an upgrade within a minute, without reattaching. The pane-is-the-subscription law's text now says the kernel never polls CUBE: a minute's read of local disk is not CUBE traffic. | `app/buildMatch.ts`, `app/status.ts`, `app/main.ts`, `calypso/client.ts`, calypso `daemon/codeIdentity.ts`, `daemon/server.ts`, `daemon/launch.ts`, menu `messages.ts`

Having them see the warning is a good thing … Does the warning currently allow the user themselves to restart?" It did not, so now it does, behind a door (`app/restart.ts`). A RESTART pill stands beside LOG OUT, and when the daemon is stale the notice's cure "Restart the calypso daemon" is a control, as Refresh is in the older-ARGUS notice. Both raise one question in the notice's spot: "Restart the calypso daemon? You'll stay logged in; the page reloads when the new daemon is up." Under it, "This will cut off:" lists only what applies now: the command holding the lane (from the heartbeat), each unsaved edit (by file), and the other surfaces (the heartbeat now lists them by id and kind, and the attach ack gives each surface its own id). YES is focused; Enter answers yes and Esc no, and neither key reaches the stage. YES posts to the door's `restart`, which answers with the greeter, and the browser follows the boot back in. Another ARGUS on the same session, told `closing` with cause restart, says "The calypso daemon is restarting (asked from another browser or surface). Following it…" and follows to the greeter itself. A page with no door shows neither control, and the notice's cure stays words. The LOG OUT pill's wiring moved to `app/door.ts` and the cycler's /bin seed to `app/cycler.ts`, which keeps the host under its ceilings (ratcheted). Proved live on a private porter, with two browsers: the question named "1 other browser"; NO left everything as it was; YES went through the greeter to a fresh daemon (new pid); the second browser said why and came back on its own; and the stale notice's cure opened the question. Law a-daemon-is-restarted-by-its-own-operator; smoke restart-question. | `app/restart.ts`, `app/door.ts`, `app/buildMatch.ts`, `app/cycler.ts`, `app/main.ts`, `calypso/client.ts`, `calypso/routes.ts`, `index.html`, `lcars/argus.css`, calypso `daemon/server.ts`, menu `messages.ts`
| 2026-10-03 (the smoke suite runs green, and says who leaked) | The operator, shown 12 reds in the epic's full run: "I don't like the 12 failed tests … the goal is to fix the underlying issue so tests can run green." The full suite now answers 317 ok, 0 failed. Four reds were tests describing an older surface: console-height dragged with mouse events on the window, after the strip moved to captured pointer events for phones; mode-frame counted the field's MORE chip as a stray control and expected a sliver strip where the spine is now drawn at `--spine-w`; and enter-place and live-watch entered a run with a single click, which has indicated since #454 (they passed only because an earlier scenario had left a graph on stage). Eight were scenarios breaking the next one, and under them were five real defects. (1) `feed rm` deleted the feed in CUBE but never told the session's index, so the RUNS roster kept listing it and a verb on that row met "feed not found"; cumin's `feed_delete` now drops it from the proc cache. (2) The RUNS pane recorded the cwd only when it followed, so the cwd went stale while the roster stood, and the next promptline after a pick read a move from minutes ago and replaced the pick; the cwd is now recorded on every promptline. (3) A follow asked before a hand pick but answered after it took the pane as if it were the pick ("chose 134, shown 21"); `features/dag/follow.ts` drops the superseded follow's answer. (4) RUNS pressed over a graph left the canvas under the roster, the old feed in the title and its watch still sampling; every way back to the roster now leaves the graph whole (`graph_leave`). (5) A feed listing brought by some other command pulled the roster up over a graph; it takes the stage only when asked for, or onto a roster. The harness now names leaks: after every scenario it checks the session is back at the suite's starting cwd, with nothing lit, no question open and no RUNS pane left on a graph. It fails the scenario that leaked, once, and puts the session back. Scenarios leave as they found it through one in-page helper (`home_return`). image-guard shows its series afresh, since LOAD is consent for one showing of a series. | `features/dag/panel.ts`, `features/dag/follow.ts`, cumin `feeds/chrisFeed.ts`, `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs`

| 2026-10-04 (an ERROR that the files contradict is not an error) | The operator, on a PACS series reading ✗ ERROR: "this seems to happen more often than not of late. I pull a series to download, and then I get some error state." Investigated: the series was 131/131 in CUBE. Two defects. The kernel's watch called a series unconfirmed when nothing arrived for fifteen seconds, and with no retries (what ARGUS runs) the step meant to ask CUBE sat inside a loop that never ran, so the series went straight to ERROR while the PACS kept sending. Now CUBE is always asked; a series not seen to finish and not yet home is UNCONFIRMED with its reason, not ERROR; the quiet window is a minute. And the pane let the wire's last word outrank a fresh answer finding the series home: an answer that finds it in CUBE now replaces a pull that ended badly (never one in flight). A badge that ended badly carries the kernel's reason on hover, and the console's summary prints it. The operator also caught the waiting animation gone: a fired pull says its total at once, so a series waiting on its first file drew a still 0% bar; it paces again until the first file lands. |
| 2026-10-04 (a silent PACS is the operator's call) | The operator, watching a test pull give up on its own: "after say 20 seconds ARGUS should, after verifying files have not landed in CUBE, pop up a 'No response yet... continue to wait Y/N'." Built so: after twenty seconds of silence from the PACS the watch asks CUBE first (a series that landed unseen is done), then asks the operator "No files from the PACS yet … and none in CUBE. Keep waiting?"; YES waits another twenty and asks again, NO ends the series UNCONFIRMED with the reason. Walked live: asked at 20 s, YES, asked again at 44 s, NO, the badge settled with its reason. Building it found the pane's PULL and query running as instrument traffic, which the session may not let ask a question — wrong, since the session's own rule is that a command changing state is never instrument traffic: the operator's acts now run as theirs (`line_execute`'s `operator`), still quiet in the console. Found on the way: pfdcm records how many instances the PACS moved for each retrieve; a series it moved none of will never arrive, and saying so outright (rather than asking to wait) is the next step. |

| 2026-10-05 (a zoom is a zoom, however the page got there) | The operator, from a files pane reached by the dashboard: opened the console, pressed its ZOOM, "expecting a full page zoom. Instead … the top header frame opened and zoom was not a real zoom." The header's slide distance was measured from its painted box, which carries the slide itself: the dashboard letting go of its zoom left the header still gliding back, it measured a few pixels, and every later zoom slid it up by those few. But a chip is a poor way to MOVE — thirty presses through a manifest — so the wheel and the finger keep scrolling, and the chip is the readout with a page-press on it. | 2026-10-01 (a refused read says why) | The operator, on a phone: an image pane reading `REFUSED 1 OF 1` and nothing else, for a file the node overlay had just listed. Now the kernel's `file_read` throws a `FileReadRefusal` carrying the status and one line (403 not yours to read; 404 no such file in CUBE; 502 CUBE could not serve it, or the kernel's own note), `/vfs` answers with that status and line, and an image pane that lost a slice asks the route why and reads it out: `REFUSED 1 OF 1 · NOT YOURS TO READ: CUBE REFUSED THE BYTES (403)`, the same line on the console. Law: a-refusal-says-why.

(2) The universe is its own pane, so the descent keeps the space's camera, memory and landings; a space re-arriving or a landing while inside is taken and drawn on the climb, never under the operator's feet. (3) Inside, a node offers what the DAG pane's node offers and nothing invented: its facts (PLUGIN, INSTANCE, STATUS, COUNT, DATA) and the verbs ENTER NODE and PROCESS on the overlay, plus OPEN FEED on the frame into a RUNS pane of its own; Esc, BACK on the frame, or `universe back` climbs out. The console subject `universe` (`enter <feed>`, `back`, `open`) is the surface's — the kernel has no `universe` command; `proc universe` is what the pane asks — and is how the smoke drives the descent, since a pointer over a settling sphere is not something a smoke can aim. A graph that arrives under a flight keeps the camera where the flight put it (`graph_set … fit: false`); the earlier `rebuild` framed every arrival and would have snapped the descent flat. Three things dragged the unfolding feed a hundred units from where it stood before the settle was made honest, each read off an instrumented flight: a root linked to its shape anchor at full strength (d3's link strength is one over the lesser degree, and a root's is one) was hauled across the cluster, so an entered feed keeps its place by its seed and links to nothing; gravity pulls to the origin, so the descent's settle runs without it; and d3's centering force shifts every free node by the mean of ALL nodes each tick, so a settle that freezes the field (every other node pinned, pushing on nothing — `graph_set … frozen`, physics for that settle alone) drops centering too. Two encodings were the cause, not the data. SIZE was linear in jobs, so an 80,000-job group was the peak, every other group sat at the smallest radius, and gravity (mass-weighted) parked the giant at the heart. A sphere's radius is now the LOG of its jobs (`jobsMetric_compute`): eighty thousand reads as a few times three hundred, not two hundred and sixty. COLOUR was the worst status, so one failed job in eighty thousand painted the whole group red. A sphere is now hued by the SHARE of its jobs that failed — done shading to error by the square root of the share, so a trace of failures still shows as a trace, red outright only when all failed — which needs one number the kernel did not send: `errored` beside `count` on a job group (cumin `pluginGroups_build`, menu, defaulting to zero from an older daemon). The tip says the count too: `pl-dcm2niix ×80,000 · 12 errored`. A SCALE block joins the frame: JOBS (the log) or FEEDS (every sphere alike; the field reads by shape alone), the way the DAG pane offers TIME and SIZE. Law: an encoding must not let one datum erase the rest — the whole point of a field is the many. |

Decisions taken: (1) the UNIVERSE is its own pane kind, `UniversePanel` on the shared scene, opened from the dashboard's tile beside the errand host and focused rather than duplicated on a second press; RUNS-02 stays a feed viewer (the wait's theater it draws while the roster is refused shares the graph and the tip, and is not this pane). (2) Hover names both what a sphere is and whose it is: `pl-dcm2niix ×300 · finishedWithError · feed 4299 · shot-cohort`, through a `tip` handler the scene now takes, since a sphere's identity should not cost a click; the feed's title rides the landing for it (cumin, menu, brasa). (3) The space BREATHES: landings keep arriving after the index is whole (a visit delta adds instances, and a landing is what that is), and a feed arriving through the roster delta or the feed count moving re-asks `proc universe` (cache-resident, at most once in five seconds), so a new feed drifts in and a gone one leaves. (4) It HUGS: a bounded repulsion reach (`PhysicsTerms.reach`, 12 units) lets one molecule hug itself instead of spreading across the field as six dots at the first landing, while a crowd still spreads. (5) It REMEMBERS: where the molecules settled is written per identity (`argus.universe.<user>@<uri>`) a few seconds after each settle and seeds the next, so a reopened pane or a reloaded page finds them where they were. Not in this slice: the pane does not survive a desktop's dormancy (its replay intent is the descent's business), the cluster has no handle yet, and a click on a sphere does nothing until slice 2 flies in. Reference: components.adoc, Universe pane. |
| 2026-09-22 (a feed lands from its leaves) | The operator, through the door at ten percent of a cold sweep: "the universe is not showing anything". Reproduced on a cold private daemon: the UNIVERSE titled `1 FEEDS · 1 SHAPES` while the canvas stayed black, and a debug read of the scene said zero nodes — the one landed feed carried no groups. The cause was in the index, not the surface: CUBE lists plugin instances newest first, so a cold sweep meets a feed's LEAVES long before its ROOT, and the landing shape was walked down from roots — empty for the whole of a 4,599-job feed until its oldest instance arrived, which for the newest feeds is the end of their block and for the oldest feeds the end of the sweep. The cache now keeps each feed's roll (every instance it holds) beside its topology, and the landing shape reads the roll: an instance whose parent has not landed stands as a root of its own until it has, and the shape settles as the parents arrive. Verified live: the first feed took its shape at 400 of 210,012 jobs — six nodes on the canvas where there had been none. Two lessons: a surface that says one thing (a feed) and draws another (nothing) is reporting a defect beneath it, and a check that copies a warm checkpoint never exercises the cold path — the earlier verification of the universe did exactly that. |

You have X feeds and XX jobs completed, with an x% job failure rate". Four decisions, all taken as recommended. (1) The greeting is the SESSION's, so it is a kernel command, `motd [surface]`, with a typed `session.motd` model beneath the text: a TTY and this console ask the same command and say the same facts (`surface-never-shadows-the-session`, `image-is-a-kernel-command`); the surface passes only its own name, since the kernel does not know its surfaces. (2) The splash is the brain at REST, `@fnndsc/menu/logo`'s static end state — the art the TTY boot and the porter's greeter already draw — not animated: through the door the greeter just animated it, and theater twice is theater. (3) The greeting is asked at attach and written with what the index holds so far — "4,700 of 210,012 jobs indexed so far", or "your index is still cold" — never reprinted later; waiting for the sweep would leave a blank console for the length of a cold boot, and the INDEX instrument carries the rest. (4) The fortune the TTY boot always showed rides inside the motd, so both surfaces end on the same cookie. The numbers cost no call: the session's own feed roster (feeds by scope, jobs by state, failure = errored over settled). The first build wrote the brain as twenty-five transcript lines and the shot showed no brain at all: the console shows about a dozen rows, so the art had scrolled itself out of sight before the greeting arrived — a splash nobody saw. The splash is now one block of two columns, the brain drawn at under half the transcript's size (eleven lines tall, `white-space: pre` so its columns hold) and the greeting beside it, the whole of it sized to the console's visible rows; the greeting lands a moment after the splash in its own column, because the prompt is its own bar. An older daemon without `motd`, or a refused ask, leaves the brain alone rather than an error. Verified live: brain 243px in a 255px console, greeting `728 feeds · 210,012 jobs run · 13.6 % failed · 0 running, 90 scheduled` beside it. |

A file called `vfs` is what the browser saves when the answer is NOT the file: DOWNLOAD handed the byte route to the browser to fetch on its own, and it saved whatever came back — the door's login page after a lapsed cookie, the daemon's `not found` — under the route's last segment. Now the page asks first with its own credentials and saves only an answer that is the file (2xx, not redirected, carrying the attachment header); the name is the header's, else the path's, never the route's; anything else saves nothing and the console says why (`download: <path>: the session could not read that file — nothing was saved`). A DOWNLOAD verb already stood on every file row — and opened the bytes in a new tab, where a browser SHOWS a CSV or a PNG rather than saving it; only a file the browser could not display ever reached the disk. The daemon's `/vfs` route now takes `download=1` and answers the same bytes as an attachment named for the file (`content-disposition`, the name carried as ASCII and as UTF-8 per RFC 6266), and the surface presses that URL through an anchor's `download`, which is the browser's own save gesture: the file lands under its own name, nothing opens. Verified live: the row verb saved the CSV on stage; through the porter the header travels the proxy untouched. The verb also stands where the file is on stage: DOWNLOAD beside CLOSE on a text file's and an image's header, so a file being READ can be taken without going back to its row (`a-control-lives-where-it-acts`); a CSV opened as a table takes it on its frame once the frame carries its verbs (#657). The kernel's `download <chris_path> <local_path>` is the terminal's projection of the same act — its disk is the engine's host — and the browser's projection writes the only disk a browser has. |

| 2026-09-21 (a surface leaves by the door it came in) | The porter lets a browser in with a cookie and mounts its session at `/s/<key>/`; the browser holds no token and asked for none. Leaving has to be as plain: a LOG OUT pill beside AUDIO and the theme, in the frame's own hue because leaving is chrome and not a state, standing ONLY on a page that came through a door (`?door`) — at a daemon's root there is no door to leave by and the pill is not drawn. Pressed, it tells the door to forget this browser (`POST …/logout`) and goes to the door's login. The session is not touched: a daemon outlives every surface, the door's own policy (idle eviction) decides when it ends, and a LOG OUT that killed the session would make "log in again" a cold boot for a colleague sharing the identity. The door's routes are found from the page's own address — two directories above the mount — so a front that puts the whole porter under a prefix moves them too. Law: a-surface-leaves-by-the-door-it-came-in — a surface that was let in by a door offers to leave by that door and by nothing else. |
| 2026-09-21 (a surface under a prefix) | The porter — the display manager, gdm to argus's DE, settled this day — proxies one identity's daemon under `/s/<identity>/` on one origin over TLS, and the browser holding an attach token in its URL bar ends with it: the door holds the token and puts it on the proxied attach. Two things in the page stood in the way, both absolute: the wire at `ws://<host>` (root, and `ws:` on an `https:` page is refused) and the byte route at `/vfs?…&token=`. Every address is now derived from where the page was served (`calypso/routes.ts`, proved under jest with a plain location): the page's directory is the daemon's mount, the socket scheme follows the page's, `vfs?…` is relative and carries a token only when the page holds one. A page sent through a door arrives at `…/?door` and attaches at once with an empty token rather than showing the attach form for something it is never told. At the daemon's root nothing changes, and the `?ws=` dev override stands. Law: a-surface-knows-its-address-from-its-page — a surface derives where its session is from where it was served, never from a root it assumes. |

So the zone filled with the row's verbs behind a frame nothing had told to open: the row lit and the verbs stayed dark, which is the same fault as the node dive's in a different disguise, and the same lesson twice over. | 2026-09-18 (a member has a kind) | Slices 3 and 4 of the gather rework, which the operator asked for in one line: "in reality the gather is a system wide collection — I can gather PACS series AND other stuff all for one purpose." A member now has a KIND — a series, a directory, a file — and GATHER stands on a browser's directory rows beside PROCESS, so a cohort holds the places a session is working on as well as the series it is fetching. The kind is what keeps the verbs honest, which is the whole reason for having one: PULL is offered only on a series that is not home, because a place is already in ChRIS and offering to fetch it is a promise nothing can keep; IMAGE only on imagery, so a folder of tables is not offered a viewer; PROCESS on anything with a place. The cohort's own bar counts what it actually holds (`1 SERIES · 1 PLACE`) rather than calling everything a series. And CREATE FEED refuses a MIXED cohort by name: a feed is rooted by PULLING its members, a place cannot be pulled, and the kernel roots a feed on one directory rather than several — so it says that, and says what to do instead, rather than rooting a feed on the series and quietly leaving the places out. That refusal is the honest placeholder for the kernel question of rooting one feed on several places. |
| 2026-09-18 (a cohort can take the stage) | Slice 2 of the gather rework. A band is right for reading and curating a cohort, and wrong for two hundred members with a filter on — so the band's frame carries ON STAGE, which puts the cohort on the main panel as a pane and retracts the band, having done its job. What makes it a second VIEW rather than a second cohort is that the set moved out of the panel: `Cohort` holds the members, the name and the feed, and tells whoever is showing it when it changed. Two panels can render one set, so removing a member in the pane empties the row in the band without either telling the other — the same rule the badge registry follows for a series shown in two places, and the answer to the operator's own framing that a gather is a reflection of one truth. A view that owned its members would make the other a copy, and a copy drifts. |

The last block of that column is not merely last: it carries the frame's elbow and its ground RUNS ON into the frame sweeping away beneath it, so appending a block after it cut the corner off the shape and left the new one wearing a radius that belongs to the frame rather than to it. The cohort's block sits between the two instead, takes the light seat between the two dark ones since it is its own thing, and carries the ordinary black rule beneath it; 02-CALYPSO keeps the corner it always had. | 2026-09-18 (the cohort rides the header) | Settled with the operator over a long grill, and the operator's argument won the shape: "the header shows the same stuff no matter where the body panel is focused. If GATHER were there, the main body panel is invariant." A cohort spans PACS series and will span directories, so it never belonged to a pane — and a companion pane rearranges the workspace on every single gather, which is a cost paid forever. It is now the header's THIRD FACE, on the mechanism the other two already use: a button selects it, the selected button pressed again sends the band away, and `data-header` carries the state. The face is a proper field — rule, elbow, spine — because a cohort is a listing and the moment it stops looking like one the rules fork: its rows' verbs ride the row zone, PULL and REMOVE ride the frame. Three decisions taken deliberately. The band is ONE height for every face, since a face that sizes to its content moves the workspace beneath it, which is the very thing this design exists to prevent. The boundary the band shares with the body is a drag strip, the console's rule read from the other side — a floor so it stays grabbable, a ceiling so it cannot crush the workspace, and the height remembered. And gathering REVEALS the band the first time, then respects a dismissal: after the operator sends it away, later gathers move only the count on the button. The cohort is written to `~/gather/current.json` as the session's own working state, so a refresh keeps it and a second surface attached to the same session sees the same cohort; SAVE still writes the named manifest, which is the artefact meant to be kept. Still to come: the pane escape for a cohort too big for a band, member KINDS so a directory can be gathered, and PROCESS made honest on a mixed set — which waits on the kernel question of rooting one feed on several places. |

| 2026-09-18 (a CSV is a listing) | The operator: "how are CSV files viewed — as a table or raw text?" Raw text, verified by opening one: a `pre` of quoted lines. Which is odd, since the file is a table produced by a table, and this surface renders every table through one façade. A delimited file now opens as a listing: its header record becomes the caps, its records the rows, and it gets the frame, the sort and the filter every other listing has. RAW stands beside CLOSE for when the quoting is the thing being read. The file is READ rather than split — a field may carry the delimiter, a newline or a doubled quote, and splitting on commas turns one such row into several wrong ones — and a first record that is not a header (a blank, a repeat, or a bare number among its cells) is kept as DATA with the columns numbered, because inventing names out of values would hide a row. The view is bounded at five thousand records and the bar says so when it bites. Two layout faults were caught by looking at the pixels rather than the DOM: hand-rolled absolute insets put the rows above their own view, and the caps row needed the same seating every other field gives it. A field is laid out the way the other fields are, or it is laid out wrong. |

That last rule is what lets a fold keep its row lit through the repaint it causes, and it is also how the #580 lit state moved into the frame: the indicated row's verbs are redrawn from their live predicates on every stage change (`indication_refresh`), so GATHER lights when the series joins the cohort and IMAGE when its viewer opens, in the zone, without the listing re-rendering — verified live, both capsules lit in turn. The model is now literal: a pane is nothing but its birth — the pane it split FROM, the split's orientation and side, and, for a pill pane, the binding it was made with — and a desktop replays by re-running each birth. This is the same shape as the shared-session idea, an action being a verb plus where it acts; the column-after default here is unchanged, and the BSP longer-axis default stays a separate follow-up. Second, the CARD FIGURE: a card showed one lone viewer thumbnail, so a PACS | DIR | viewer desktop read as just its viewer. The figure is now a STRIP of the whole set — each shown pane drawn as a tile in on-screen left-to-right order (sorted by each pane's measured position, NOT creation order, since a viewer opened before a browser can sit to its right — the first cut walked creation order and drew a PACS | DIR | viewer set out of order while restore, which reads the captured placement, put it back right) at a width proportional to the width it held on screen, a raster where the pane has a canvas and a kind glyph where it does not (a listing, tags) — so the card reads as the arrangement it restores. Restoring replays those lines, so the viewer's own open splits it beside PACS exactly as it did live, and the whole desktop returns. Cards are keyed by the on-stage viewer's series (recognisable, deduped — returning updates the one card); a bare domain with no viewer is reachable from its gutter button and is not carded. This unifies with the existing `desktop` feature — a card is a desktop, auto-captured on leave, and `desktop save <name>` still pins one. The narrow-PACS clip (a series row wider than a half-width split pane) is resolved where it lives, in rendering: the workspace scrolls in both axes rather than clip its last verb, and replay inherits that for free. Opening PANES sends the current group dormant, which is free and recoverable: it is itself a card in the grid. It is a domain, not a floating overlay, because LCARS has no floating chrome and the gutter already teaches "press a domain, the stage becomes it"; the overlay's one merit, not disturbing the current stage, is moot when leaving is recoverable. | 2026-09-15 (a group that leaves the stage is dormant, not gone) | Slice 2 of pane-group restoration. A gutter domain switch ran an orphan sweep that DISPOSED every non-primary pane — the image viewer, its tags, any split — engine and mount, permanently; only the three primaries survived off-stage. That is the "lost panes" defect: nothing to return to. Now the sweep first SNAPSHOTS each leaving link group into a dormant set (`app/dormant.ts`), then disposes its live panes. A snapshot is not the pane; it is what a replay needs — the series or volume the group regarded (its stable id, one series is one card), the image view state (layout, slice, window/level, colormap, ghost, now exposed through `state_get`), the member kinds, and a small raster of the viewer. | 2026-09-15 (a PACS viewer is its series' group) | First slice of pane-group restoration (design: pane-group-restoration.md). Opening an image from PACS spawned the viewer with no host group — an anonymous loner the pane drawer could not reach, and a second IMAGE on the same series spawned a duplicate. A viewer opened from a pane that has a group (a files row) already joined that group; the PACS path did not, because it opens with no originating pane. Now that path anchors the viewer's OWN group on the series it shows (the group's regard address is the series folder, `dicom.series`), so the group IS that series' group: the drawer reaches it, and a viewer already on stage regarding the same series is reused rather than duplicated. One series is one group, whichever PACS pane spawned it. This is the foundation for the group as the unit of restoration — a group will be a replayable transcript of the intents that built it, retained dormant when it leaves the stage and brought back from a PANES gutter view, so a navigation away never loses the arrangement. Later slices carry the dormant lifecycle, the PANES domain, and a DIR row verb. |
| 2026-09-15 (a text surface shows the image, not only its facts) | Following on from image becoming a kernel command: a TTY sharing the session saw the series' facts but no picture, so the reflection now carries a thumbnail of the middle slice. The kernel reads that slice's pixels (`dicomSlice_gray` in salsa, uncompressed transfer syntaxes via the dcmjs it already loads), applies the file's window/level or an auto range, and normalises to 8-bit with MONOCHROME1 inverted so bright always reads high. brasa box-downsamples that grid to a cell width — correcting for the ~2:1 aspect of a character cell so the image is not stretched — and renders it one of two ways, chosen by a new declared surface capability, `color`: an ASCII ramp (` .:-=+*#%@`) that any pipe can print, or ANSI truecolour half-blocks (`▀`, two pixel rows per cell) on a colour terminal. ARGUS declares `color: true` in its attach, because the DOM console renders ANSI; chell reads its own terminal through chalk; a bare pipe gets the ramp. Compressed pixels (the JPEG family, RLE) need a codec the kernel does not carry, so the reflection says so in one line rather than pulling wasm into the kernel; a slice that cannot be read at all leaves the facts and no picture. The plain ramp is always the floor. Verified live on the isolated daemon — the ASCII ramp on a piped chell, truecolour half-blocks on a colour one — and in units (`salsa` decode/window-level, `brasa` the two renderings and the compressed and unreadable paths). |

It was drifting the other way — `image` had begun to grow surface-only help and no place in the kernel, the exact link violation the workspace model forbids. So `image <path>` is a kernel command now (`packages/brasa/src/builtins/res/image.ts`): it resolves what the path is — a series folder, a `.dcm`, or a NIfTI/MGZ volume — and emits a typed `image.view` intent (`IMAGE_MODEL_KINDS.view`, schema in `@fnndsc/menu`) that every surface renders in its own way. ARGUS opens a rendered pane from the intent (`envelope_observe` calls `image_open`); a TTY prints the reflection the command renders, which for a series is the same facts `dcm series` shows under an `image` line, so the terminal answers the identical `image` with text where the browser answers with pixels. The surface keeps only the subverbs that drive a pane already on the field — `image layout|slice|wl|colormap|save|tags|load|guard|ghost` — declared in `SHARED_SUBJECTS` so a bare `image <path>`, `image --help`, and `image` alone all fall through to the session; the surface no longer shadows the command (`surface-never-shadows-the-session`). Help lives in the kernel's own registry, so `image --help` reads on a TTY and in the console alike. Verified live on the isolated daemon and in smoke (`image-answers`): a typed `image <path>` opens the pane through the intent, a bad path is refused in the console by the kernel, and a brasa unit covers the command's grammar and reflection. The TTY reflection is a line of facts today; an ASCII rendering is noted for later. Law: `image-is-a-kernel-command`. |
