| 2026-09-10 (a run follows itself) | The operator, after starting a plugin: "when a new run happens, I'd expect telemetry to appear in the header". It did not, and the reason was worse than a missing push. The heartbeat is a second long and was working; what it reported was a cache that never learned. A new feed enters the cache with its jobs SCHEDULED, which is what they are at that instant, and nothing revisited them unless a pane happened to be open on that feed — so the operator's own run never turned over to running, never turned over to finished, and RUNNING sat at zero while SCHEDULED only ever climbed. That operator's header read `SCHEDULED 90` with nothing running: not a gauge, a tally of everything they had ever started. The sampler already existed and already knew when to stop, since a settled feed cannot change; what was missing was anyone starting it when nobody was looking. A run now takes out its own watch, released when the feed settles. Measured live: RUNNING went 0, then 1 IN 1 FEEDS while the root ran, 1 again for the child, and back to 0 — and SCHEDULED rose by one and came back down instead of staying up. Where the watch is taken matters and was got wrong first: putting it in the cache-writing helper made every test that records a feed start visiting CUBE, which is the helper surprising its callers. A run is followed where a run is STARTED. The historical backlog those 90 represent is NOT fixed by this and is its own defect: entries stranded as scheduled before anything followed them. |
| 2026-09-10 (a series is known by its name or its place) | The operator had a folder of DICOM files of their own and no way to open it from the browser, though `image ~/upload/SAG-anon` opened all 192 slices from the console the whole time. The verb was offered on a NAME alone — oxidicom writes a series folder as `<number>-<description>-<7 hex>` and nothing else does — which is a good signal and an incomplete one. Two things were put aside on the operator's call. Offering IMAGE on every folder, because a verb meaningful in a handful of cases is noise in the rest: "I don't like adding a verb to every selection that is meaningful in only a handful of cases." And looking ahead into each folder to decide, because that is one request per row before the operator has pressed anything — the same cost that made a replayed PACS query feel like a fresh one earlier the same day. What was taken instead is the operator's own: inside `/SERVICES/PACS` the PLACE says what a folder is, and depth is free to read because the row already holds its path. So a series is known by its name anywhere, or by sitting at series depth in the PACS tree, whatever it is called there. Verified live against the hospital tree: a series row inside a study offers IMAGE, a patient row at the server level does not. The folder outside the tree still has no verb, which is the trade the operator chose. |
| 2026-09-10 (a dive that opens nothing strands the diver) | The operator entered a node and got what they read as a crash screen: the pane filled with one flat colour, nothing moving, nothing to press. Nothing had crashed. Entering a node flies the camera to just shy of the node's surface — arrival is meant to read as passing inside — and the arrival callback then mounts the node's own filesystem over the scene. If that second half declines, it declined SILENTLY, and a silent decline is not a cancelled dive: the camera is already inside, the scene is held so even the idle spin stops, and the operator is left looking at the inside of a sphere with no way out. Two ways in existed. The pane may hold a record of an overlay whose DOM went with a rebuilt pane, a ghost that then refused every later dive on that pane; ghosts are dropped now rather than believed. And a decline for any other reason now flies the camera back and says on the console why. Esc is also a way out from anywhere: a camera held inside a node with nothing over it answers Escape by flying home, whatever left it there. The operator's third question was the sharpest — why no test found it. Because none had ever dived. The files pane's inline diagram has a dive of its own and the smoke checks it, which made the gesture LOOK covered; that one immerses a facts chip and never mounts a filesystem, so the DAG pane's dive, the whole second half of the animation, had no check at all. It has one now: touch a node, dive, and assert the node's own listing arrived and that Esc leaves the node while keeping the scene. |
| 2026-09-10 (a rebuilt surface has to arrive) | The operator restarted the daemon after a merge and reported the new control simply absent: "I see no ANON pill anywhere on the PACS Q/R?". It was in the built bundle; it was not in their browser. The daemon served the web root with a content type and NOTHING else — no `cache-control`, no `etag`, no `last-modified` — and silence there is not neutral: a browser is then free to guess, and it guesses that it may keep the page. `index.html` is the file that NAMES the hashed assets, so a kept copy pins the whole surface to the build that wrote it, and every rebuild after that arrives nowhere with nothing on either side saying why. The bundle is two kinds of file with opposite needs and now says so: `index.html` is `no-store`, because it is small, it is the index of everything else, and it must never be the stale part; a hashed asset is `immutable` for a year, because a different build is a different URL and this copy is therefore good forever; anything else revalidates, which is the safe answer for a name that can be reused. Verified on the wire, both headers read back from a running daemon. The lesson is the general one: shipping a surface is not the same as delivering it, and the delivery had never been tested. |
| 2026-09-10 (a column of bars is read as a column) | Operator review of the series level, from a screenshot: the progress bars were "jagged on right". They were, and the cause was in the cell rather than in the bar — the bar and its state note shared one flexible track, so every bar stopped where its own note began, and `NOT RETRIEVED` beside `✓ 74 IN CUBE` tore the right edge into steps. The note has a track of its own width now and every bar ends on one line, measured across 21 series. That width is measured rather than guessed in both directions: the longest thing a series ever says is a retrieve in flight, `RETRIEVING 1410/1760` at 120px, so a track cut to the resting states would clip the note at the one moment an operator is watching it — and everything past that is bar the operator does not get, so it is not rounded up generously either. Two more from the same review. A count is read against the counts above and below it, so FILES is set flush right and its cap follows it there. And EXPORT CSV sat in the GATHER strip, where SAVE and CREATE FEED both act on the cohort, while it alone acted on the whole answer — placement saying the opposite of what the control did. There are two now, which is what the operator wanted once the ambiguity was named: the answer's export stands on the results frame beside FILTER, on the field it exports, and the cohort gets `EXPORT GATHER CSV` beside the cohort's own verbs, naming its narrower scope and composed the way SAVE composes its manifest. Three smoke checks hold the geometry, since a torn edge is exactly the kind of thing every test stays green through. |
| 2026-09-10 (a demo need not name a patient) | Demonstrating against a live hospital PACS puts a real name, record number and date of birth on a projector, and the operator asked for a way to query without that. ANON, a pill beside the command line, arms a kernel that stands in for every identifying value before an answer leaves it: the record number and accession become salted tokens, the name becomes a stand-in name of the same DICOM shape, a study date falls back to the first of its month and a birth date to its year. Stood in for on the DECODED payload, which the model, the table, any CSV and the console text are all built from, so one substitution reaches every one of them and none of them has to know. The salt is made once per daemon and never written down: a record number is seven digits and an unsalted digest of that space is enumerable in under a second, so tokens are stable through one demo, meaningless after it, and a pairing leaked from a recording says nothing about any other. The DICOM identifiers are deliberately left real — they are never shown, and they are what the held-state reconciliation matches on, so standing in for them would break an answer while hiding nothing. Three findings shaped the surface. The typed line is the first identifying thing on a screen, so the pill masks the form fields AND the command line, which spells the same values back; the line still reads exactly what runs, keeping the pane's standing claim, it is simply not readable across a room. Masking a field an operator is filling hides their own typos, and a query put to a hospital PACS for the wrong record number is worse than a visible one, so REVEAL lifts it. And the pill arms the NEXT query rather than toggling a view, because the substitution happens in the kernel: rows already on stage were fetched one way or the other and cannot be turned, so arming clears the answer instead of leaving rows whose state nobody could name. What it does not cover, said plainly: the files browser after a pull still shows CUBE's own folder name, which carries the record number, the name and the date of birth. |
| 2026-09-10 (a replay asks by study, not by series) | The operator, on a historical MRN: it "took 15+ seconds .. that is NOT what a cached restore should be and felt 100% as if it searched the PACS again". It had not searched the PACS. The stored answer came back at once and its date was honest; what took the time was the reconciliation that follows — asking CUBE, for EVERY series in the answer, whether it already holds it, at two round trips each. Measured live on a patient's history of 299 series in 20 studies: 20.6 s, and 6.8 s for a 14-series accession, with the whole answer held back until the last of them replied. So the one thing a replay exists to avoid, trouble on the wire, is what a replay spent its time on. CUBE indexes a stored series by its STUDY, so the same reconciliation is one page per study — a count of tens, not of hundreds — and file counts are a second pass over only the series actually home, since the series record carries the folder but no count. The same two questions now cost 3.1 s and 1.5 s at the kernel, measured the same way. The operator's own proposal, a cached mirror of the `/SERVICES/PACS` tree, was put aside for a reason worth recording: `folder_path` is built from names and does not carry the SeriesInstanceUID, so a folder cache cannot answer "is this series held" without the series records anyway — and with no change feed from CUBE, a long-lived mirror of a tree that grows with every pull cannot be invalidated honestly. The records ARE the index; they only had to be asked for in one breath. Held by unit tests that pin the QUESTION COUNT rather than the answer, because an answer built the slow way looks identical. |
| 2026-09-10 (an abandoned question is not a failure) | The operator asked what they see if they `rm` something and then press Esc, and the two answers turned out to be different sentences. `rm -rI` — what the row's DELETE verb runs — caught the rejection and said `nothing removed (1 kept)` in grey, an ordinary outcome; `rm -i` awaited the same question with nothing around it and said `rm: cannot remove 'x': the operator abandoned the question` in red, an error over a file nothing had touched. Under `-i` over a list it was worse than inconsistent: the rejection left the walk, so every file behind the abandoned one was silently never offered. Both paths now treat a question that came back with no answer the way `a-question-is-answered-where-the-session-speaks` already says they should — the command is told, and reports what it did not do. `-i` skips that file by name and stops asking, because an operator who backed out of this question is not waiting to be asked the next nineteen, and it says `2 more not asked about, and kept` rather than leaving a set silently alone. The reason travels onto both lines, so a surface that has quietly lost its voice does not read as an operator who declined — the old `-I` catch swallowed the two cases into one. Verified live on all three shapes, every file still there afterwards, no error line anywhere. Also here: a stray `|||||||` conflict marker had been sitting in this table since #499, drawing a garbage row. |
| 2026-09-09 (bars say what happened, quietly) | Operator review of the RUNS roster. A finished feed drew a full bar in the bright done hue, and most DAGs finish, so the roster read as a wall of bright success — loud where nothing is wrong, drowning the running feeds a live roster is for. The done fill takes the recessive dark tone the ID/owner/created columns wore (`--pumpkin-pie`, a dark blue on MEDICAL), and those columns take the progress shade (`--daybreak`) in trade — so the roster's mass of finished bars sits back and a running feed's brighter bar is what the eye catches. And an errored feed drew a full bar in the error hue, which says nothing a red mark would not; it now fills only to the work that succeeded — `successfulNodes / totalNodes` — in the error hue, so the bar's length is how far the feed got before it died (48738 of 58760 then errored is an 83% red bar, not a full one). The failed count rides the wire as `jobsErrored` on the feed-list model, from the proc cache's own per-status counters, and the fill falls back to the settled count when a daemon does not report it. Verified live: 480 done bars at alpha 0.34, 225 errored each at `round(succeeded/total*100)%`.
| 2026-09-09 (the boot ends at a login) | The daemon face is retired. An interactive `chell --daemon` now ends its boot the way the call-out does: once the daemon is listening, an ordinary `chell --remote --attach` surface is put on the daemon's own terminal, so the boot log runs straight into a prompt. It is a child on the wire (the daemon's sink and surface singletons are never shared), `exit` detaches and Enter attaches again, Ctrl-C at the idle terminal stops the daemon, and the daemon's own writes while a surface holds the terminal are held and handed back on detach. The same slice lifted the boot readout: deferred steps settle as `[ OK ]`/`[FAIL]` instead of an open `[PENDING]`, every count agrees with its noun (`count_noun`), the stack banner writes every package out in full with an ARGUS row (`ARGUS Renders Graphical User Surfaces`, coined here) and drops the welcome, and a daemon bound on every interface advertises its fully qualified name. Settled section: _The daemon console_.
| 2026-09-09 (the lab's pulse, and a readout that acts) | Epic #495, S6. `RUNNING 480 IN 12 FEEDS · SCHEDULED 3` joins the INDEX instrument: the lab's pulse, derived once a second from the roster's own job counters (kept current by the visit delta and the roster sync, so no call is made) and carried on the telemetry heartbeat (`state`). Its ERRORED figure is the one press on the face that acts: a capsule in the errand's hue that opens the runs roster filtered to the feeds with errored jobs (`status:error`, the listing's own filter, which holds across the rows arriving), the pane put on stage first so the press acts on what the operator can see. That needed a law of its own beside `a-control-lives-where-it-acts`: `a-readout-that-acts-says-so` (smoke). | 2026-09-09 (the lane, the beat and CUBE's pace) | Epic #495, S5, from the creative think the operator asked for after the INDEX instrument landed. Three rows join it on the ARGUS WEB face, each changing what the operator does. LANE: the serialized command queue as a gauge — `IDLE`, or the line holding it, for how long, and how many wait behind it, red past ten seconds; the daemon carries it on its telemetry heartbeat (`lane`), counting the queue as commands enter and leave it and naming the running line, an instrument command never counted as holding the lane. This is the gauge the stall that opened the epic had no reading on. BEAT: the age of the last heartbeat on the surface's own clock, red past three seconds, the daemon's health lamp (the heartbeat defect of #484 would have shown at once). CUBE: the pace CUBE serves pages at — the median of the last twenty page fetches, timed as they pass through cumin's one `resource_call` — carried on the heartbeat (`cube`), and the number that turns a walk's page count into time: every FEED row on the INDEX instrument now reads an ETA, from the walk's own observed rate once it has moved and from CUBE's pace with the window of four before that. Smoke `lane-instrument`: IDLE and a fresh beat at rest, IDLE while a feed indexes off the lane, a pace once pages were fetched.
| 2026-09-09 (the roster is index movement) | Epic #495, S4, found while accepting S1 live: with a large topology walk running off the lane, a RUNS-02 press sat BUSY for over ten seconds, because `proc feeds` awaited the roster's sync — the delta, or the ten-minute full walk of every feed the identity can see — inside the foreground command, and CUBE was slow under the concurrent walk. The roster is index movement like the rest: `proc feeds` and `proc jobs list` now answer from the cache at once and START the sync (`procRoster_syncStart`, deduped with a sync in flight); the walk runs off the lane; the prompt context names it while it runs (`procWarmup.roster`, delta or full) so the status line reads ROSTER WALK, the INDEX instrument shows a ROSTER row, and chell's prompt says `[roster: full walk]`; and arrivals land as they did, bringing the pane back for the rest (the roster it answered with may be a few feeds behind for the seconds the walk takes, which the instrument says). The boot-time sync and the restore reconcile still wait, as warm-up steps do.
| 2026-09-09 (four pages in flight) | Epic #495, S3. Measured on the hospital CUBE: about 2.8 s per 100-row page, so a 58,760-node feed walked sequentially for 28 minutes (35 under the smoke suite's load). The per-feed topology walk now fetches four pages at once: the first page alone, since it tells the server's total and so every further offset, then a window of four, each page yielded as it lands. The consumer keys rows by id and commits the whole set when the walk lands, so arrival order is nothing to it; the global sweep keeps its sequential loop, because its resume-by-offset cursor needs pages in order and a contiguous-prefix commit is its own slice. A server that reports no total is walked one page at a time as before. Four is PACS plural's precedent against this CUBE (#400); eight is untested on the shared hospital instance and not tried without a measurement. cumin's `listPages_walkWindowed` is the one pagination loop, windowed, with tests for the window's width, the no-total fallback and a failing page; salsa's `FEED_WALK_IN_FLIGHT` names the width.
| 2026-09-09 (index movement is read on the header) | Epic #495, S2. Index movement was annunciated on the prompt and on the status line as text that vanished when it was done; the operator asked for it to be surfaced clearly in the header pane, the original warm-up included. The INDEX instrument now heads the ARGUS WEB face — the live telemetry beside LINK, JOBS INDEXED and OPS, where the operator corrected a first placement on the resting face: that face carries the versions, and a tracker among version numbers "makes no sense". Permanent: a quiet index reads `95,989 JOBS · 705 FEEDS · CURRENT` (a quiet daemon says so; a blank would be a lie), the global sweep rides that row with a bar and its state, a feed's first-visit walk takes a row of its own (`FEED 2384 · INDEXING 6,500 / 58,760`, a bar that follows the count; `FAILED AT n / m · reason` while the failure is remembered), arrivals a third (`ARRIVED FEED 21 · FEED 22`), and a warm-up step that failed behind the prompt keeps the index row red with the steps named. One source drawn twice: the same prompt context the status line reads, and the telemetry heartbeat's steady counts; the bars are the listing façade's progress idiom in the state hues, never a raw colour. Building it found a gap the full smoke had just shown red: the prompt context carried ONE feed walk (the earliest in flight), so a second walk — the suite's `proc refresh 834` beside the operator's 2384 — was invisible on the status line, on its pane, and on the instrument. The context now carries every walk in flight (`procWarmup.feeds`, earliest first; `feed` stays the first for older readers), and the status line, the pane, the instrument and both chell prompt themes name each one. Seven jsdom tests compose the rows from the context; smoke `header-index` reads CURRENT, watches a feed's row appear with its bar during `proc refresh`, and sees it go. Proving S1's kick live found two more: the pane's repaint path dropped a sampler's graph while the pane was in its pending posture (the landed graph now completes the wait as the answer to the ask), and `proc refresh <feed>` removed the feed's roster row and re-read it from the own-feeds endpoint only, so a feed seen through the public listing came back "not found" after its re-walk (the refresh now evicts the topology and keeps the row, re-reading it from own then public feeds); a refresh also no longer carries the `feed.indexing` model, since a refresh is not a request to view and its answer must not claim a pane.
| 2026-09-09 (index movement never holds the lane) | The measured stall the 2026-08-31 row waited for: entering a never-indexed feed of 58,760 nodes showed the annunciated count warming (6,500/58,760, so the earlier law held) while a PACS query and everything else spun for the minute it took, because the daemon runs every command through one serialized lane and `feed diagram` awaited the whole 588-page walk inline in it. Eight decisions, grilled: the cold walk is index movement, beside the global sweep and the visit delta, not a background-job facility (those stay deferred); `feed diagram`, wire `feed.dag` and `proc refresh <feed>` answer at once with a typed `feed.indexing` envelope on every surface, chell printing one line and its prompt segment carrying the count; a started walk is never cancelled, and a failed one names where it stopped for a minute and retries on the next visit; completion kicks the feed's watch, which holds the floor while pending and never settles on an empty topology, so the graph publishes within one build; the pane shows the walk in place (INDEXING FEED n with the live count, FAILED with REFRESH retrying). The blocking command was removed rather than prioritised, exactly the condition the old row set, so express-lane queue priority stays shelved. Owed in this epic: the header INDEX instrument on the resting face (idle CURRENT, one row per movement with a bar), four pages in flight in the per-feed walk, and — found while accepting this slice live — the roster's ten-minute re-walk inside `proc feeds`, which held the lane for over ten seconds while CUBE served the topology walk (#499). Measured on the hospital CUBE: about 2.8 s per 100-row page, so 58,760 nodes walk in about 28 minutes sequentially, which is what the four-in-flight slice is for. Law `index-movement-never-holds-the-lane` (smoke).
| 2026-09-08 (PHAROS, the house visual language: the lighthouse, flat) | The operator liked PHAROS's first light and asked for it with medical colours. Two builds went wrong before a line was kept, and the record of why is in `pharos.adoc`: a token swap on the imported frame read as the imported look in beige, and a frame without the imported look's mass read as buttons in dead space. The finding is a law of taste rather than of code: the imported look's chrome IS its structure, and a language that subtracts that mass must put an equal mass back. Three studies did (the continuous run chamfered, the reading room's lightbox, the lighthouse's tower); the tower was chosen, flat, since the bevels of the first pass looked obsolete on arrival. The grammar settled over five rounds of mocks: stone (flat colour, lit band, black joint), cut (the 45° facet on a body stone's outer corner and small on every capsule), inset (the focused pane's gutter stone tucks in from the outer edge, a binder tab; the variant that slid it across to the room was rejected as waste), lens (the one curve, the header stone's foot where the elbow stood, a Fresnel lens round a breathing lamp: distinctive, and an homage), flank (the outer silhouette rounded at its two corners behind a wall, found by accident in a rounded thumbnail), course (bars ending in a point, square junctions, the gallery rail). Built as `data-theme='pharos'` on the existing frame; the one TypeScript change is that the layout now declares the focused pane on the body as `data-focus`, so the frame answers focus without being told. The imported look stays the default, in MEDICAL; PHAROS wears MEDICAL's seven colours in this slice. `data-lcars` renamed `data-theme` since it names a theme and not a franchise, with the remembered choice migrated; face tokens `--face-label`/`--face-data` replace twenty-seven literal font names.

It does not — not its source, its tests, its reference section, its stylesheet block, its changesets or its status rows — and that was deliberate rather than enforced. Now it is enforced: `a-component-names-no-theme` fails CI when the word appears in `features/roster/` or in the component reference. | 2026-09-08 (PACS declares its levels) | Epic #428, S4, the last conversion. The PACS pane's three levels — patient, study, series — are one declaration into the façade: a child declared through `listingChild_declare` beneath a child, each level with its own traits and tracks, its verbs standing on every row, one filter read down the levels with a parent surviving when a child matches, one sort naming its level. What the pane composed by hand leaves it: three orders, a host, two expansion sets, two render methods, the fold glyphs, the group wrappers, the state line. The façade gained what the conversion needed and nothing it did not: a row that heads a level is wrapped with it in a group that opens and closes as one thing, a level carries its depth so the stylesheet indents by depth rather than by pane, rows can be held open programmatically (a level of one row opens itself), a block set with nothing in it can say so, and a form can ask for a child level's template — which the workspace now carries from the façade rather than from the stylesheet. The four remaining hand-typed track lists are gone; the lint's allowance for this pane, the last, expired with them. Not carried by the façade, deliberately: the summed progress bars and the per-series badges stay the pane's, painted into mounts its own cells register, since a live per-cell channel is not a listing concern. Verified against a live PACS: the query, the fold, the sort inside a study, the provenance readout, the server strip, and the bars still lined up level over level.
| 2026-09-08 (reaching past the façade is the violation) | Epic #428, S5, taken before S4 at the operator's call. `listing-is-one-abstraction` now names the thing it protects: a pane reaching past the `Listing` façade — building a `RosterOrder` or `ListingHost`, calling the row or capsule builders, importing the order or the host, or spelling `--roster-cols` by hand in the stylesheet — fails the gate. The PACS pane is the one allowance, named in the lint and printed as debt on every run, deleted with S4; the four track lists it still declares are listed by selector for the same reason. The dead grid rules the two converted panes left behind (`.files-grid`, `.feedlist-row`'s grid) are gone, and `roster-grid-single-source` reads the façade's `.listing-row` in their place. What the gate cannot yet say is that S4 is done; what it says from here is that nothing else can regress.

Both had regressed. The series level was designed with its bar beside the description; the patient level and the reshaped study level then put PROGRESS after every fact, at the far right, and each level on a grid of its own — measured, the patient bar began 277 px left of the study's. All three routes did it: `mv`'s own PUT, `cp`'s upload of its copy to an occupied name, and — the one that made the original rows — mise's own compensation for CUBE's collision rename, which PUT the wanted path back onto a file whose name was genuinely taken. The kernel now builds a model first and renders the manual from it, so `plugin info pl-dcm2niix-v2.0.0` and `cat /bin/pl-dcm2niix-v2.0.0` say the same facts rather than scraping twice and risking disagreement. `plugin.info` (menu) carries identity, the authoring facts, and every declared parameter with the flag as an operator types it — a plugin's declared `flag` when it has one, `--name` otherwise, because a form built from the name alone would spell `--inputFile` where the plugin wants `-i`. The parameter list is drained to exhaustion: the `getPluginParameters({ limit: 100 })` this replaces was one of the silent truncations catalogued in #401, and a parameter table that stops at a page boundary is worse than no table, because it still looks complete. No surface change yet — this is the wire fact a plugin's one-node graph will read.
| 2026-09-06 (a node opens as what it is) | The operator asked that clicking a node in a pipeline dive in and show `text : detail` pairs, parameter on the left and value on the right. It needed no kernel work at all: `pipelineDiagramNodeSchema` has carried `pluginName`, `pluginVersion` and `arguments` since the model was written, so both the touch and the dive read what already arrived. A touch reads the node out — plugin, version, and how many arguments there are to see; a double-click flies the camera in, as the runs pane's dive already does, and the readout becomes every argument in full. Esc flies back out, one level: out of the node, not out of the view holding it, which is the retreat-exactly-one-level rule the chain has followed since Esc learned about content views. Two small refusals worth naming: a graph at rest says nothing, because a panel of facts nobody asked for is the wall of text this epic exists to remove; and a node with no authored arguments says "none — this node runs on the plugin's defaults" rather than showing an empty panel, since emptiness reads as a failure to load. Law `a-node-opens-as-what-it-is` (smoke).
| 2026-09-05 (the wave, asked for) | The operator wanted a node wave on the `/bin` pipeline view and was asked whether it should breathe or be pressed; they answered "keep the law.. have node wave be user triggered", so PULSE fires one pass on a press and the control reads as the verb. Building it exposed something older: the `/bin` detail had no chrome of its own, but the files pane's mode frame is a SIBLING of the panel's container, so a pipeline's DAG sat on stage while the frame still offered LIST, CARDS, PREVIEW and FILTER — four controls that could not act on a graph. The fix is not a second frame but a truer reading of the one there is: a pane has ONE frame and its blocks answer to what the field currently holds. A listing offers its projection and filter; a diagram offers RANKED/MOLECULE, 3D/2D and PULSE; closing the diagram gives the frame back. The bar annunciates the non-default modes as it does everywhere else. And the wave replays DEPENDENCY order here rather than execution, because a registered pipeline has never run — same animation, different claim, and the pill does not pretend otherwise. Laws `one-frame-answers-the-field` and `a-wave-is-a-verb` (both smoke).
| 2026-09-05 (one layout, two renderers) | The operator opened a pipeline's preview card beside its detail view and said they were "different shape/form", which they were: the card spread each tier evenly while the stage hung a tree of leaf slots with each subtree centred over its span. Nobody chose that. They were written separately — `pipelineSvg_build` in the files panel, `layout_ranked` in the scene — and could not help but differ. Placement is now declared once in `scene/rankedLayout.ts`, dependency-free, and drawn twice: three.js on the stage, SVG in the card, which cannot hold a WebGL context because a grid wants ninety of them and a browser grants about a dozen. What the scene keeps is the third dimension and the metric scaling; what the card keeps is fitting layout units into a viewBox. Being free of three.js, the layout is also the first part of argus a unit test can reach at all — determinism, sibling order, a join taking the deepest of its parents, and a cycle still landing — so argus gained a small jest setup alongside the smoke suite, which needs a live daemon and a real browser. Law `a-preview-is-the-same-drawing` (lint), which fails a renderer that computes its own tiers.
| 2026-09-05 (an answer says where it came from) | A PACS query is the slowest interactive thing in the surface, and CUBE has been storing every answer all along — 1,817 of them on the measured CUBE — so a question asked before need not be asked again. Epic #379 built that, and the operator settled what it looks like: almost nothing. Replay is INVISIBLE. You type a query and results come; when they have been seen before they arrive at once. Nothing exposes the log, the index, or a query id — an earlier draft proposed surfacing the query log as a browsable listing and was rejected, correctly, for making an operator learn a mechanism that exists to serve them. What changes is two things and no more: a provenance READOUT appears in the form's PROGRESS track, immediately left of the button, reading `RESULTS 2026-06-14 · 3 MONTHS AGO` — the date is the fact worth quoting into a note, the elapsed time the judgement worth acting on — and QUERY becomes RE-QUERY in `--october-sunset`, the deeper shade PULL STUDY already wears, never `--mars`, which is refusal. RE-QUERY lowers to `pacs query … --fresh`, which appears in the editable line because everything in this pane is a command the operator can read; when the fresh answer lands the pill clears and the button reverts, so the control always reads as what it will do next. A fresh answer carries no readout: the absence is the statement. The readout was first drawn as a capsule and the operator caught it — a capsule is a thing you press, and one that answers no press is a promise broken, however lawful a filled pill is elsewhere in the grammar (the status strip's ONLINE and HOST lamps are exactly that). The lamps get away with it by living where nothing is pressable; this sits beside RE-QUERY, so it wears the frame's caption type instead of the capsule's costume. Shape is role. Staleness is stated and never decided — an accession names a study that will not change, an MRN can gain one tomorrow, and rather than classify query shapes by heuristic the surface says the age and leaves the judgement where it belongs. Law `provenance-is-annunciated` (smoke, needs `SMOKE_PACS_QUERY`).
| 2026-09-05 (one listing, three panes) | The operator's own reading: "listing objects in the UI are multiple sources of truth when they should be centralized". Files, runs and PACS each spelled the same table — caps, sort, filter, rows, verbs — and a fix to one reached none of the others. Epic #385 pulled them onto one abstraction in four slices; this row closes it with the payoff. The PACS results are now a framed field like the other two: the frame outside the scroll, a mode frame on the spine carrying FILTER, `pacs sort` and `pacs filter` in the language. Its columns are declared once as traits, its PULL and GATHER are actions outside the column grid (a verb answers to no cap), and its levels FOLD — a study keeps its place and opens its series inside it, where a directory or a feed REPLACES the level above. Both levels are listings: the operator's first look at this said "I didn't see any col headers", and they were right twice over — a study was a run-on label rather than a row, and an accession, the number a report is filed under and the one an operator arrives holding, was buried in the middle of it. The STUDY caps now head the region before any answer arrives (PATIENT, MRN, STUDY, DATE, ACCESSION, MODALITY, SERIES, PROGRESS), and a study's SERIES caps are minted inside it when it unfolds. The filter reads down the levels: a study matching on its own columns keeps all its series, a study that does not keeps the series that match, and a study with neither leaves the stage — so an accession gives a whole study and a modality gives the series. One order state governs every study on stage, so a sort touched in one lights and orders them all: `RosterOrder` learned to mint a caps row per group. Progress now reads at every level, summed and never averaged — a study's bar is its series' addition, so one finished series of ten files cannot outweigh a stalled one of ten thousand — and a series nothing has been asked of carries a dim track reading NOT RETRIEVED, because an absent bar reads as "no such thing" where a dim one reads as "nothing has happened yet". Also found live: the same answer can arrive twice, and the second arrival was throwing away the fold the operator had just opened — a repeat of a query now keeps the folds and the retrieves in flight, and only a new query resets the pane. The operator then asked whether the search bar should follow the same order and layout, and it should: the form now stands on the STUDY grid in the STUDY caps' order (PATIENT, MRN, —, DATE, ACCESSION, MODALITY), QUERY sits where PULL STUDY sits, and the editable `pacs query ...` line it generates reads left to right as the listing does. STUDY gets a gap rather than a field: `StudyDescription` reaches the PACS and the PACS ignores it — measured, both studies came back including the one that does not match — so it is an optional C-FIND key a server may drop, recorded in `docs/CUBE-gaps.adoc` and offered as a column but never as a control. Law `a-form-stands-where-its-answer-will` (smoke). The remaining level is the patient: an MRN-level listing folding down to studies, which needs the query model to carry patients (epic #379). Caught while verifying live: `pacs sort` and `pacs filter` joined the language, and `pacs` is also a kernel command — the surface swallowed `pacs query` and answered "unknown verb". A shared subject now claims only the verbs the session lacks and falls through for the rest, under law `surface-never-shadows-the-session` (lint), which reads the kernel's own dispatch table so the next collision fails CI instead of an operator's query. Laws `listing-is-one-abstraction` (lint) and `progress-sums-its-levels` (smoke, needs `SMOKE_PACS_QUERY`).
| 2026-09-04 (warm-up leaves the gate) | The boot readout blocked on four prefetches in front of the prompt. Measured against a live CUBE: `/PUBLIC` costs 8.4s and `/SHARED` 9.3s — eighteen seconds an operator spent watching a prompt that was already theirs, buying freshness the stale-serve path delivers a moment later anyway. Now boot blocks only on `/bin`, which completion cannot work without; Groups, Feeds, Public and a newly added `/SHARED` step warm behind the prompt under a new `[PENDING]` boot status. `/SHARED` had no step at all before — nothing to fail, so a CUBE that stopped serving shared paths stayed silent until somebody went looking. A deferred step leaves the boot failure gate, so its failure can no longer stop a daemon binding; it is instead held in the kernel until a later attempt succeeds and carried on the prompt context as `warmupFailures`, said by chell's prompt (`[warm-up failed: groups]`) and named on argus's JOBS readout in mars. Named, not counted: "Groups" tells an operator which capability is degraded where "1 warm-up failed" only tells them to go looking. Law `deferred-warmup-failure-persists` (smoke). Doctrine home: this row and the boot-cache epic #367.
| 2026-09-03 (index movement) | The operator asked what signal says a newly shared 20k-node feed is being indexed, and when it is done, and whether that belongs in a header pane. Traced: the global sweep already reached the prompt and the JOBS readout, but a feed's first-visit load — the 20k case — emitted nothing, and a feed the roster gained (created, or shared to this identity) arrived silently. Now the kernel keeps two more registers on the process cache (one feed's load in flight; roster arrivals for thirty seconds) and the prompt context carries them on the same `procWarmup` segment (`feed`, `arrived`, and `sweeping` so a renderer can tell a sweep from a load). Chell's prompt says `feed 812 indexing: 3400/20000 17%` and `+feed 812`; argus's JOBS readout says `FEED 812 3400/20000` and `+FEED 812`; a roster on stage pulses the arrival's row and re-lists when it does not yet hold the feed. The daemon's heartbeat keeps pushing the promptline while the segment is present, so the count climbs with no command typed. Law `index-movement-is-annunciated` (smoke, needs `SMOKE_BIG_FEED`).
| 2026-09-03 (the runaway canvas) | The operator reported the /bin pipeline view "regressed": text, no diagram, no CLOSE, a horizontal scrollbar. Not reproducible headless — and that was the lesson: headless Chrome runs at device pixel ratio 1, a desktop at 1.25 to 2. The scene sizes its canvas in DEVICE pixels and never writes the CSS size; the DAG pane and the header cycler each pinned the canvas with a stylesheet rule of their own, the /bin diagram mount had none, so at any ratio above 1 the canvas's device width fed back into its mount's width on every resize until it was 33 million pixels wide, pushing CLOSE and the graph out of view. Fixed at the source: the scene tags its canvas (`.dag-scene-canvas`) and one rule pins every mount. The smoke driver learned `SMOKE_DPR` (CDP device-metrics emulation) and the suite runs green at 1.5×; the bin-context scenario asserts no sideways overflow. Law `canvas-fills-its-mount`.
| 2026-09-03 (host control) | Grilled with the operator, eight decisions: the daemon may declare capabilities of its own, the AI-harness permission model — `chell --daemon --host-control[=shell,files,pipes]`, off by default, host always wins under it, refused on a network bind without `--expose-host-control`, `sh -c` with stdin closed and output on the bus, `upload` refuses without the `files` tier, surfaces learn it from the attach ack. Argus's part: the status strip's amber HOST lamp (annunciation, never at rest), read from the ack's `hostControl`, and the refusal text for `!` on a daemon without the policy now names the flag. The `edit` relay is a different thing — argus needs an editor of its own (#356, CodeMirror). Law `host-control-is-annunciated` (smoke). Doctrine home: `docs/session-supervisor.adoc`, "Host control".
| 2026-09-03 (hue is a mode) | The operator asked whether pipeline glimpses should rotate and whether nodes should be colored by compute assignment. Rotation: no — the dark cockpit animates nothing at rest, ninety spinning thumbnails is noise, and the header's ambient cycler is the one deliberate exception. Compute: yes, where the data exists. CUBE fixes a job's compute at run time, on the plugin instance (`compute_resource_name`), not on the pipeline's pipings — so a registered pipeline's glimpse has no compute to show and stays monochrome, while a FEED does. The process cache now records each instance's resource (cumin + salsa, from the list row it already reads), the `feed.dag` node carries it (`mixed` for a group whose members disagree), and the DAG pane's mode frame gained a HUE block: STATUS (default) or COMPUTE, which colors nodes from the frame's own hue cycle in order of first appearance, errors still winning, groups that ran on several resources in grey — and the legend travels with the mode as swatches on the bar readout (`COMPUTE ▪host ▪galena`). The block dims when no node of the feed reports a resource. `dag hue status\|compute`; a desktop saves it. Law `hue-is-a-mode` (smoke).
| 2026-09-03 (totals, targets, glimpses) | Three from the operator. The feed roster now carries SIZE and TIME — total output bytes and the wall span from first node start to last node end (to now while running) — derived by the kernel from resident nodes with no wire (`feedTotals_derive`; an 88k-node feed is 88k additions), and only where they mean something: the RUNS roster, never a CFS listing such as `~/feeds/feed_N`, which is a directory and not a run. A feed whose topology is not resident reads a dash, never a zero. A link now says where it points (`→ target` beside its name in LIST, its own line in CARDS), and entering one works: `cd` on a CFS path ending in a link had gone to the real-folder validator, which asked CUBE for a folder at the link's own path; it now follows the link's target, and a refusal names that target. And PREVIEW in /bin: a plugin's glimpse is the head of its description (through the session's `cat`, since /bin has no bytes to serve), a pipeline's is its authored graph drawn small — tiers by depth, no physics, an SVG that scales to the card, and a node count instead past eighty.
| 2026-09-03 (nits, and why there were so many) | The operator asked why the frame kept shipping with nits. Honest answer: each pass was verified by geometry and by crops of the corner it changed, never by looking at the whole composition against the canon — so a hover that flipped to near-white, three frame members in one flat hue, a focus ring cut by sticky children, a lamp whose black type lost to the strip's readout rule, and two roster grids spelled twice all passed. Fixed together: a hover BRIGHTENS a control in its own hue and never glares (law `hover-never-glares`, lint); every segmented frame member — caps, mode blocks, the bar's fill — cycles the frame's three hues (harvestgold, daybreak, orange), as the gutter does, so columns and modes read apart without gaps; caps are bar segments with a uniform 3px of black whatever their grid gap, so the files and runs rosters look alike; the roster grid is declared ONCE per host (`--roster-cols`, `--roster-gap`, `--roster-pad`) and read by caps and rows both (law `roster-grid-single-source`, lint); the focus ring rides a veil above sticky caps and headers; the lamp's rule outranks the strip's; and a node's extensionless files (log, params, status) preview as text until their head proves binary.
| 2026-09-03 (the bar) | Second look from the operator, three screens: the blocks beside the spine still read as floating buttons of a new kind, not frame; the node's browser inside the DAG had no frame at all (unscoped caps wrapping OWNER, the canvas elbow peeking past it); a hard-coded orange focus ring in sickbay; NAME jammed against its cap's curve; a native scrollbar; ONLINE in green-on-green. All fixed: the spine now WIDENS into the elbow's vertical bar when touched, and the blocks are that bar's SEGMENTS — labels on the bar, black gaps between, a plain fill to the foot — so the open frame is unmistakably frame, costs field width (the incentive to dismiss it), and narrows back to the spine on touch, fill, field, or Esc. The node browser is stamped from the files template (same frame, same caps grid, scoped to any files body), and the canvas frame steps aside while it is up. Every translucent frame color derives from the theme through `color-mix` (rings, glows, dim blocks), the caps' ends carry padding matching their radius, scrollbars are thin frame-colored bars on black everywhere, and the lamp wears black type.
| 2026-09-03 (the frame, drawn) | The operator looked at the first mode frame and saw no frame: a black gradient on black with floating pills, and an elbow stub joining nothing. Not LCARS — the frame is solid. Settled with the operator: every field with modes is framed at rest by a RULE along its top (the files pane: the path header's rule, thickened, the header made sticky under the caps so the rule never scrolls away; the DAG canvas and the feed roster: a rule under the bar) turning through a real ELBOW (fat, inner radius cut in black) into the SPINE down the right edge. The join is static — nothing has to line up during a slide. Touch the spine and the MODE BLOCKS slide in under the rule, flush against the spine, stacked with the sidebar's thin black gaps — the column is the frame, no panel. The scroller sits left of the spine. And the filter: doctrine said a mode summoned on demand, the code hid it by default, and the CSS (`display: flex` beating `hidden`) had it standing on every roster since the caps landed — the smoke had read the attribute, not the geometry. FILTER is now a block on the mode frame (files and runs), reading its state; the drawer's FILTER capsule retired (one control per mode; `file filter` / `runs filter` lower to it); the strip folds out under the rule and the smoke measures it folded at rest and open after the block. Law rows unchanged; the caps-are-the-sort statement now names the block.
| 2026-09-03 (the mode frame) | The operator caught the LIST / CARDS pill sitting in the files pane's field, lower right, and asked why the gate had not — and the honest answer was that doctrine had legislated it: the 2026-08-31 rail rows moved the DAG's mode pills "off the header bar into an in-pane rail", and the files pill copied that. The rail's TAXONOMY was right (display-content modes, apart from the drawer's machinery); its PLACEMENT was a web-map control in orange — nothing floats over the field in Okuda's grammar, controls live on the frame. A frame column at rest failed the arithmetic (a capsule column is ~100 px; a pane with one pill would give up 95 % of it to empty frame), the drawer alone would hide state and cost a gesture per flip. Settled with the operator by riffing on a law already on the books: the header gets out of the way and leaves a strip; a pane's MODE FRAME does the same at pane scale. A thin strip at the field's right edge at rest; touch it and the frame slides in on its elbow with the mode capsules; touch the field (or Esc) and it retracts. Open on touch, never on hover (hover only lights the strip: every affordance by touch, and hover-open would collide with orbit and drag); it overlays the field as a transient (never reflowing the 3D buffer) and joins the Esc chain beside the drawers; the same edge on every pane that has modes, and no strip on a pane that has none; the bar annunciates every non-default mode (`MOLECULE · 2D · CENSUS`, `CARDS`) so state stays legible with everything retracted, and nothing at rest. The language is untouched — `dag projection 3d` lowers to the same capsule. Laws `field-is-content` (lint: every mode pill inside a mode frame, no control in the DAG or files field, and the repealed rail's CSS deleted — a repealed rule left standing enforces the repeal's opposite) and `frame-retracts-to-a-strip` (smoke). The 2026-08-31 rail placement is repealed; its taxonomy stands.
| 2026-09-03 (PREVIEW, corrected) | The 2026-09-02 cards row read PREVIEW as listing-plus-selected-file and held it as a split verb. Wrong: the operator meant the file manager's icon view — every entry drawn with a rendered glimpse of its content. Built as the third projection on the files mode frame (LIST / CARDS / PREVIEW, `file preview`): a card that leads with a thumbnail — an image served natively from the token-gated `/vfs` route, the head of a text file (a bounded read of the same route, the body cancelled once 600 bytes are in — a preview never pulls a whole log), or the kind's glyph when nothing renders (a directory, a plugin, a DICOM file, a text file over 256 KB). Fetched only when the card scrolls into view (IntersectionObserver over the panel), heads remembered per path. The slaved VIEWER remains what it was: a split with a viewer binding.
| 2026-09-02 (cards) | The operator asked for a card view of the Files pane, pointing at the prototype's `AssetCard`. Built as a projection mode on a files rail (LIST / CARDS, the pill reading the current mode like the DAG's): the same listing, the same caps sort and filter strip, the same activation (a plugin card opens its description, a directory card enters). Cards carry the kind as a colored badge, the name, owner and date, size. A preview mode was NOT added as a third projection: listing-plus-selected-file is the slaved VIEWER that a split already gives, so PREVIEW will be a verb that performs that split (held). Law `projection-is-a-mode`; `file list\|cards`. _Superseded 2026-09-03: PREVIEW is the thumbnail projection, and the rail is the mode frame — see the two rows above._
| 2026-09-02 (the language, written down) | The operator asked for the visual action space as a document. `docs/argus-lang.adoc` is the long form of `argus verbs`: grammar (`subject [@id\|%n] verb args`), every subject and verb with what it lowers to (drawer capsule, rail pill, gutter given, DOM event, or session command), the desktop script format, and the completion vocabulary. Rule stated there: a verb, its help line, the doc, and the ledger move in one PR. Found while writing: `argus verbs` omitted `runs sort\|filter`, `file sort\|filter`, and `node clear`; fixed.
| 2026-09-02 (a follow answers a move, not a promptline) | Operator report, the real one: feed 2583 opened and a few seconds later the pane showed feed 21. Cause: the session cwd was parked inside feed 21 (left there by a test on the shared daemon session), and the cwd-follow treated every promptline whose cwd feed differed from the pinned one as "a different feed underfoot", replacing the pick as soon as the pane's own silent command pushed a promptline. The follow now acts only when the cwd actually changed since it was last observed: a pin survives until the operator cds elsewhere. The smoke restores the session cwd after moving it.
| 2026-09-02 (a turned tree is still the tree) | Operator report: dragging the background of BLT-TEST-3 "jumped to a different feed". Reproduced headless: same feed, same title — an empty-space drag orbits, and the ranked tree carried a per-node depth jitter (a parallax gimmick from the tier-spread days) that, seen from the side, scrambled the hierarchy into an unrecognisable graph. RANKED is now a sheet, every node at z = 0: an orbit turns the sheet and the tree stays the tree. Orbit in 3D and pan in 2D are unchanged.
| 2026-09-02 (a files row says what it is) | Operator catch: the Files pane had no kind column — a name alone does not say plugin, pipeline, link, directory, or job. TYPE joins the grid between NAME and SIZE, sortable like every column, caps and rows on the same template.
| 2026-09-02 (Esc pops a content view anywhere) | Operator catch: Esc did nothing over a /bin entry view. The contextual-back chain knew node overlays and DAG levels but not a files pane's own content view. Now a files pane's content (a file, a /bin entry) is a level in the chain — the focused pane answers first, else whichever pane has content up — so Esc returns it to its listing exactly as CLOSE does.
| 2026-09-02 (/bin rows open as context) | Operator ask: browsing /bin, a plugin or pipeline row did nothing. Now a plugin row opens `cat` of the entry — the description grammar highlighted (headings, `Key:` labels, flags, quoted values; ANSI from the daemon rendered as sent) — and a pipeline row opens its summary with the `pipeline diagram` model rendered in a DagScene beneath it (flex-filled work surface per the desktop contract), disposed on CLOSE. Same content view, same Esc level, as a file. Law `bin-is-browsable`.
| 2026-09-02 (the console's browser, declared) | The operator rediscovered a binding they had designed and forgotten: the primary browser follows the session cwd. Correct default (terminal plus file manager), wrong legibility — same chrome as a rooted split-born browser, no readout, no verb. | 2026-09-02 (Esc pops the file view; ranked is a tree) | Two operator nits. (1) Inside a node, Esc on an open log left the whole node — contextual-back applied: a file view is a visible level, so the first Esc returns to the node's listing and the next leaves the node; directory depth stays BACK's (invisible depth is never walked by Esc). (2) RANKED spread each tier's nodes evenly and sorted by id, so children landed nowhere near their parents and edges zig-zagged across the pane; the old ChRIS UI's tree read at a glance. RANKED is now drawn as the anchor tree: every node beneath its first present parent, a subtree over a contiguous span of leaf slots with its root centred on the span, joins drawn as extra edges without moving anything. Same determinism, relationships read straight down.
| 2026-09-02 (caps are a frame, and frames stay put) | Operator review of the roster caps: too thin, labels right-aligned over left-aligned cells (read as misaligned), a real user's name in the filter hint, and the question of whether caps scroll with the rows. Settled: caps are the roster's frame — taller (24px), each label aligned exactly as its column's text (ID and the numeric file columns right, the rest left, no padding the cells lack), anchored (`position: sticky`) with the filter strip while rows scroll beneath, measured cap-to-column geometry exact on both panes. Hint text names no one. Also caught: a fresh page whose cwd sat inside a feed had RUNS-02 land on the roster and then repaint the cwd's feed over it — the cwd-follow now yields while the roster (or a selection in flight) is on stage.

Now ENTER NODE resolves a place (a file regard becomes its directory, a node regard its data, none the feed on stage), the roster pick writes the feed's address as regard (`modelKind: feed`), and ENTER FEED sits beside ENTER NODE for the session-global move into the feed. Honest-wait applied to selection: the roster steps aside at the click, the pane reads RETRIEVING FEED N…, the bar reads LOADING (butter, no animation — one animation author is CSS and this needs none), Esc backs out of a selection in flight, and a refusal returns the roster with the reason above it. Law `select-answers-at-once`, smoke-enforced.
| 2026-09-02 (listings survive a restart, honestly) | The fifth PR of the live-/proc campaign: directory listings are checkpointed (identity-keyed, throttled) and restored with their original timestamps, so a restored listing is served STALE and revalidated behind itself — the Files pane's bar says STALE until the session's refresh arrives on the ambient bus and swaps the listing in place. Found and fixed on the way: the listing path served any cached entry regardless of its TTL, so a listing never refreshed until eviction or `ls -f`; now a fresh entry serves, a stale one serves-and-revalidates where a host can carry the refresh (the daemon), and refetches in line at a plain console.
| 2026-09-02 (the pane is the subscription) | Grilled with the operator: a dynamic pipeline grows on CUBE while the DAG pane watches two nodes forever — the old UI's reload problem in a new place. Settled the kernel half in three PRs: `/proc` freshness became VISIT-DRIVEN (a revisit is a delta — feed counters, then only the nodes created, finished, or still running since the cursor; settled feeds re-checked per ten minutes; the roster picks up newer feeds on every `/proc/jobs` visit and walks whole once it is ten minutes old); the checkpoint became per-feed SHARDS with a per-shard write floor (a growing feed rewrites only itself); and liveness got its own noun — a WATCH (`watch`/`unwatch` wire pair, owned per surface, released on detach; `proc watch|unwatch` at the console) drives an adaptive sampler (3 s while changing, 30 s when quiet, stops on settle) whose refreshed `feed.dag` models ride the session bus from the `daemon` surface, with `watched` reporting live / settled / stale. Regard stays what it is: what the operator indicates, not what a surface keeps open. Surface half built in the fourth PR: the DAG pane opens its watch on entering a feed and releases it on leaving (Esc, RUNS-02, close); daemon-originated models refresh the pane in place (same groups and counts patch status; anything else re-lays out warm, so the census re-expands only when a count moved) and never reach the transcript or pin; the bar reads LIVE / SETTLED / STALE; REFRESH is a drawer capsule (`dag refresh`); the roster re-asks the session once a minute while it stays on screen. Law `pane-is-the-subscription`, smoke-enforced.

| 2026-09-02 (every pill a question, every knob a sentence) | Grilled: should the physics terms be rail pills? No — the rail carries MEANINGS (which layout, which metric, shape or census), and charge/link/collision are the implementation of an honest layout; a pill to switch collision off is a pill to request interpenetrating lies. Expert knobs belong to the language: `dag physics charge|link|collide|gravity on|off` and `dag physics reset` reach the pane through DOM events (verbs run over the DOM, never a private API). Mass is the exception — "heaviest at the heart" is a meaning an operator wants or not — so mass-weighted centering earned the GRAVITY ON/OFF pill (rail convention: label = current state, dim when off). And the node detail got its off switches: a click on empty space clears it (the natural gesture), the DAG drawer offers CLEAR DETAIL, and `node clear` says it — the operator could not find the selected member again in an 88k cloud to click it off.
| 2026-09-02 (radius is physics, not paint) | Operator insight, upheld: the molecule sim was metric-blind — uniform charge and link distance, the radius painted on afterwards — so a 43-hour stage and a 4-second one claimed identical space. Now the metric IS the physics: repulsion scales with cross-section (r²), links reach surface to surface (rₐ+rᵦ+base), and a collision force at 1.2r forbids interpenetration. TIME and SIZE therefore reshape the molecule, not just its spheres — verified on 2583: TIME a downward cascade with the core held high, SIZE a starburst around a central core. Re-projections warm-start from the standing positions (fewer ticks), so a metric flip morphs the graph instead of re-rolling a new equilibrium. Census inherits it: shell radii follow the anchor radius. Mass-weighted centering (heaviest stage at the heart) deliberately deferred until the operator judges the three terms.
