Page-scroll formally ceded: the composition is a declaration (preset + header face + drawer height), scroll position is hidden state, and content depth lives inside instruments.
| 2026-08-31 (yield order + select=enter) | Two corrections from live use (PR #277): the desktop contract gained its yield law — when header + console outgrow the viewport the DRAWER shrinks, never the workspace, which keeps a 20rem floor (the frozen drawer had crushed panes to a sliver); and selecting a feed in the RUNS-02 list now ENTERS the full graph (list steps aside, Esc returns) — the half-pane preview-above-the-list is gone, with live scrubbing reserved for a future context split.
| 2026-08-31 (console face, settled) | Third pass locked the shape (operator correction): the boot stays the classic in-scroll experience — brain lit at connect, boot log and in-stage progress beneath — and at ready the screen is replaced by a MINIMAL face on the alternate buffer: the animating brain, one status line (DAEMON RUNNING · SESSION ESTABLISHED / ENGINE EXECUTING), and the Esc hint. Nothing else. Esc = the verbatim text record (boot log, addresses, token); any key returns. The ready handoff halts the boot pulse without a final repaint, so the text record is never stamped over. Earlier two-phase/panel design (PRs #271/#273/#274) superseded by PR #276.
| 2026-08-31 (desktop contract) | The workspace declared a desktop, not a document (PR #272): the shell owns the viewport (100dvh, page never scrolls), the main region flexes into what the header and console leave, every pane fills its leaf as a flex column, and scrolling happens inside instruments — never on the page. The fixed viewport fractions (42vh interior caps) are gone; a full-workspace preset really is full-workspace.
| 2026-08-31 (contextual back) | Esc reframed as an Android-style contextual back: per-pane navigation stacks (feed list → graph → node immersion), one pop per press, transient chrome (drawer, then zoom) before navigation; preview replaces the stack top while dblclick-enter pushes; RUNS-02 declared as always landing on the pure feed roster (no ghost renders). Navigation macro language (text commands driving the same intents from the embedded terminal) sketched, gated on its own grill.
| 2026-08-30 (pulse + fly-in) | Two DAG behaviors grilled and locked: the pulse wave (dependency-order replay; a join fires only when all parents have; history-honest — the wave halts at the execution frontier; drawer verb plus one wave on load, always-on only in the ambient miniature) and the fly-in (dblclick dollies the camera into a node and overlays a rooted browser in the same pane; Esc exits the node in one press; the overlay shares the DAG pane's group so regard writes feed its slaved viewers; ENTER NODE stays in the drawer as the session-global cd).
| 2026-08-30 (PHAROS) | LCARS grounded properly (what it is, why chosen, whose property). Longevity strategy settled: PHAROS house language as default (chamfer, beacon, harbor palette; recursive name — Presentation House for AEGIS — and the six-chapter inventory), LCARS demoted to operator-supplied skin via theme import, functional carve-out grounded in _Lotus v. Borland_ / _Apple v. Microsoft_. Naming law recorded: kitchen names never expand; harbor names expand through the layer below, grounding at CALYPSO's fixed point; ARGUS coined the All-Regarding Graphical User Surface.
| 2026-08-30 (slice 1 built) | Regard + drawer + slaved viewer implemented: `regard` wire message (menu), brasa session retention, calypso retain/mirror/rebroadcast with late-attach replay, argus SubjectBus (groups, retained cells, LWW), pane drawer behind the header on all stamped panes, VIEWER semantic child (slaved) and BROWSE NODE (rooted) in the drawer, viewer pane kind subscribed to group regard.
| 2026-08-30 (later) | Subjects grilled and locked: two-layer regard (group-scoped rendering + session aggregate in brasa), retained-cell write semantics, rooted-versus-slaved children, lifecycle (groups session-only, session regard survives surface reload). Durable layer settled in direction: per-user config and named layout schemas as CFS files under `~/.config`. Build of the first slice (regard skeleton, pane drawer, slaved viewer) begun.
| 2026-08-30 | Document created from the first AEGIS thought sprint. Settled: the machinery-behind-the-frame principle (pane drawer as the mouse-native prefix key), semantic children as parent-contextualized pre-composed intents, pane linkage as hub-and-spoke subjects (never pane-to-pane edges), the addressability test, and *regard* as a session-truth subject in brasa. Open: palette roles, shape grammar, motion canon, the violations audit.
|===

== The laws (machine-checked)

The components a pane declares into are documented one section each in `components.adoc`, the reference beneath this doctrine. Doctrine without teeth decays into apology. Every law below is enforced mechanically: `lint:` checks run in `scripts/aegis-lint.mjs` on every pull request; `smoke:` scenarios run in `apps/argus/tests/smoke/smoke.mjs` against a live surface. The lint gate also verifies this table itself — a law whose named enforcement does not exist fails CI, so new doctrine cannot be written without teeth.

[cols="1,3,1"]
|===
| Law | Statement | Enforcement

| drawer-everywhere | Every workspace pane — template-stamped or static — carries the pane handle and the three-group drawer. | lint:drawer-everywhere
| bar-no-machinery | No machinery rides a title bar: no buttons, roles, or control text inside a bar beyond the handle itself. | lint:bar-no-machinery
| caps-are-punctuation | Every bar end-cap is passive frame punctuation: the punctuation class, no text, no role. | lint:caps-are-punctuation
| lid-is-mute | The lid's segments carry no text — the lid is punctuation, and the whole lid is the toggle. | lint:lid-is-mute
| single-animation-author | An animation has one author: CSS declares it; script never stamps an animation-bearing class. | lint:single-animation-author
| lid-parity | The lid is identical open and closed; only the end block may breathe, and only while closed. | smoke:lid segments identical
| zoom-immersive | A zoomed pane owns the stage: header, gutter, lid, and status offstage; only the thin restore strip remains, and it restores. | smoke:zoom slides the whole header
| given-idempotent | A gutter given always renders its target; it never toggles. | smoke:a given never toggles
| control-reads-next-action | A toggle control reads as what it will do next (ZOOM/RESTORE, open/close). | smoke:the capsule reads RESTORE
| a-wait-over-two-seconds-shows-progress | Anything that changes the surface and takes more than two seconds shows its progress where it happens: a bar that moves, or — when the end cannot be counted, as a question to the session — the words for what it is waiting on and a running bar. The work runs in slices, so the page can draw the bar; a wait with nothing on screen reads as a dead surface. | smoke:a wait over two seconds shows its progress
| honest-wait | A wait or refusal is always visible and truthful; a loading row never outlives its request. | smoke:RUNS-02 answers
| touch-first | Every affordance works by touch: no keyboard-only control. | smoke:LANG opens the line
| ordinals-mark-channels | A gutter ordinal (`-0N`) marks a summonable domain channel, numbered sequentially down the column; meta givens (LANG — the language, not a channel) carry none. | lint:ordinals-mark-channels
| a-click-does-not-resettle-the-space | Indicating a node changes only the node's paint: no click re-settles the graph or refits a camera the operator has placed; a repaint of the same space keeps that camera. | smoke:a click keeps the camera
| focus-citizenship | The prefix key addresses whatever the operator last touched — and the console is a citizen of that model like any pane. | smoke:focus citizenship
| caps-are-the-sort | A tabular pane's column caps are its own frame: touch a cap to sort, again to reverse, the active cap lit; the frame stays anchored while the rows scroll, and each cap's label sits where its column's text sits. Filtering is a mode summoned from the mode frame's FILTER block or the language, and the title bar carries its state (`FILTERED n/m`). A grouped listing heads each group with its own caps, minted from the same declaration and lit together: one sort, one filter, however many groups are on stage. | smoke:column caps sort
| console-joins-the-grammar | The console is a pane that does not split: the same handle and drawer as every pane, with only the verbs that apply — no SPLIT, hence no binding. | smoke:console drawer
| nameplate-seal | Marks are engraved seals in the nameplate's own black — never full-color logos on chrome. | smoke:nameplate seal
| select-answers-at-once | A selection answers before its data does: the list steps aside, the pane names what it is retrieving, and the bar reads LOADING until the answer lands or the refusal shows. | smoke:selecting a feed answers at once
| enter-lands-in-a-place | ENTER moves the session into a directory, never a file: a regarded file resolves to its directory, a regarded node to its data, nothing regarded to the feed on stage; the roster pick offers ENTER FEED. | smoke:ENTER FEED moves the session into the feed
| bin-is-browsable | A /bin entry opens as context, not a dead row: everything there is a graph — a pipeline as its summary with its authored DAG, a plugin as the one-node graph it is — both on the same stage, with the same CLOSE a file view has, and no wall of scraped text anywhere. | smoke:a plugin opens as a graph of one node
| a-view-is-closed-by-the-operator | A listing arriving for a pane whose operator is reading something lands UNDER it; CLOSE and Esc then show the newer one. A view taken away mid-read loses whatever was fetched into it, and a click that opens a view is not a request to close it. | smoke:a view is closed by the operator, never by an arriving listing
| projection-is-a-mode | A listing pane draws its one listing in more than one way — rows, cards, or cards with previews — and the way is a mode-frame capsule that reads as the current state; sort, filter, and activation are the same under every projection. | smoke:CARDS projects the same listing
| following-is-declared | A browser bound to the session cwd says so on its bar (`CWD`); the binding is a verb both ways (FOLLOW CWD / ROOT HERE, `file follow` / `file root`), so any browser can be the console's and the console's can be frozen. A binding nobody can see is a hardcode. | smoke:the following browser says CWD on its bar
| pin-survives-promptlines | An operator's pick holds until the operator moves: a cwd-follow answers a change of cwd, never the promptline every command brings, so a session parked inside one feed cannot replace a pick of another. | smoke:a pick survives promptlines while the cwd sits elsewhere
| hover-never-glares | A hover brightens a control in its own hue; no hover rule reaches for the palette's near-white. | lint:hover-never-glares
| roster-grid-single-source | A roster's columns, gap, and edge padding are declared once on its host and read by caps and rows alike; no roster grid spells its own template. | lint:roster-grid-single-source
| canvas-fills-its-mount | A scene canvas fills its mount in CSS pixels at any device pixel ratio: the scene pins its own canvas, never a per-host stylesheet; a content view never spills sideways. | smoke:the content view never spills sideways
| host-control-is-annunciated | A daemon acting on its own host is never silent about it: the HOST lamp reads the attach ack's declared tiers, present exactly when declared, absent at rest. | smoke:the HOST lamp reads the daemon
| hue-is-a-mode | What colors a node is a declared mode on the mode frame (STATUS, COMPUTE), and a mode's legend travels with it on the bar; errors are visible under every hue. | smoke:the HUE block reads STATUS
| field-is-content | A pane's field carries content only: display-mode capsules live on the mode frame, no control floats over the field, and the repealed floating rail's enforcement is gone. | lint:field-is-content
| deferred-warmup-failure-persists | A warm-up moved off the boot gate reports its failure afterwards, by name, and keeps reporting it until a later attempt succeeds — a boot readout has scrolled away by then, and a message that vanishes is one nobody acts on. | smoke:a failed deferred warm-up is named on the status readout and persists
| index-movement-never-holds-the-lane | A command that needs a topology the cache does not hold answers at once that the feed is indexing, and the walk runs off the session's serialized lane: every other command from every surface goes on answering while a large feed indexes, and the graph follows on the feed's watch when the walk lands. | smoke:a cold walk returns the lane at once
| index-movement-is-annunciated | Index movement no command announces — a feed's first-visit load, a roster arrival, the global sweep — reaches the JOBS readout and the prompt while it happens and clears when it is done; a walk of a minute never reads as a hang. | smoke:the JOBS readout names a feed being indexed
| frame-retracts-to-a-strip | A frame that gets out of the way leaves a thin strip — the header, a pane's mode frame — and touching the strip brings it back; touching the field or Esc retracts a mode frame; the bar annunciates the non-default modes meanwhile. | smoke:the mode frame rests as a strip
| surface-never-shadows-the-session | The argus language runs before a line reaches the session, so a subject that is also a kernel command must claim only the verbs the session lacks and declare itself shared; every other subject collides with nothing. A surface that eats `pacs query` has taken the operator's instrument away. | lint:surface-never-shadows-the-session
| a-node-opens-as-what-it-is | Activating a node opens what it is made of, in the surface's own `text : detail` idiom, and Esc retreats exactly one level — out of the node, not out of the view holding it. A node that carries nothing says so, because an empty panel reads as a failure to load rather than as a thing with nothing in it. | smoke:diving in opens the node as its parameters
| one-frame-answers-the-field | A pane has one mode frame, and its blocks answer to what the field currently holds: a listing offers its projection and filter, a diagram offers the modes a graph has, and neither offers the other's. A control that cannot act is a promise the frame does not get to make. | smoke:a diagram on stage swaps the frame
| a-wave-is-a-verb | Motion is asked for, never ambient. The dependency wave runs one pass when a hand presses it and the control reads as the verb, because the dark cockpit animates nothing at rest — the header's ambient cycler remains the single deliberate exception. | smoke:PULSE is a verb
| a-preview-is-the-same-drawing | A preview and the thing it previews are one drawing at two sizes: the placement is declared once and each medium only paints it. A card may paint SVG where the stage paints three.js — a grid of ninety previews cannot hold ninety WebGL contexts — but it may not compute its own tiers. | lint:a-preview-is-the-same-drawing
| provenance-is-annunciated | An answer that was not fetched now says when it was — the date it was answered and how long ago — and the control that would replace it reads as what it will do next, lowering to a command the operator can see. A fresh answer says nothing, because the absence of the claim is the claim. The surface states the age and never acts on it. | smoke:a replayed answer says when it was answered
| a-form-stands-where-its-answer-will | A query form's fields sit on the listing's own grid, in its caps' order, so a term is typed in the column it will fill and the line it lowers to reads as the listing reads. A column the query cannot narrow on carries no control at all — a field that silently does nothing is worse than a gap. | smoke:the query form stands on the listing grid
| listing-is-one-abstraction | A tabular pane declares its listing into the `Listing` façade and never builds one: columns are traits declared once for cap, cell, order and track; a row's verbs are actions the façade seats in the track it reserves; the frame that sorts and filters, the indication, the readout and the selection are the façade's. Reaching past it — to the order, the host, the row or capsule builders, or a hand-typed `--roster-cols` in the stylesheet — is the violation, since that is how three panes came to spell one table three ways and then one indication twice. The PACS pane carries the one named allowance, printed as debt on every run, until S4 converts it. | lint:listing-is-one-abstraction
| a-selection-belongs-to-the-field | SELECT is a mode: while it is on a click gathers rows and row verbs stand down. The selection is the field's — it survives a filter and a re-listing, it is cleared by navigation, and leaving the mode keeps it — and its verbs ride the frame's row zone, each lowering to ONE command over many operands that asks once for the whole set. | smoke:a bulk act is one command the operator could have typed
| a-row-is-indicated-before-it-is-acted-on | A listing that hides its verbs until a row is indicated must distinguish indicating from activating: a click indicates, a double-click activates, and a row with no verbs to hide — or a listing with none — keeps its single click; a row with a control keeps navigating apart from selecting: the control activates (folds, enters, opens) and selects nothing, the rest of the row selects and does not activate. Where the verbs stay on the row, the track that holds them is reserved on every row at one declared height, so indicating changes what the track holds and never the geometry around it; where they ride the frame there is no track to reserve; and every row verb lowers to a session command the operator can read. | smoke:no row moves when one is indicated
| process-acts-on-a-place | PROCESS is offered on a place — a directory outside a feed, a node's own data inside one, a DAG node, a PACS series or wholly-home study — never on a file, never beneath a node. It opens `/bin` as a catalogue bound to that place, joined to the pane's group; RUN there lowers to the one line the console would take, echoed, with a new feed's title asked once. | smoke:PROCESS opens a catalogue bound to the place, and RUN schedules a run on it
| a-row-s-verbs-live-in-the-frame | A row's verbs are drawn in the frame's row zone, never on the row: a listing that declares the zone reserves no track, the indicated row is drawn as a segment of the frame (its block colour, black type, run to the spine), verbs arriving open the frame and the last of them leaving retracts it unless the operator holds it, and the frame retracting stands the row down. A listing holds one indication across its levels, and it belongs to the field: a repaint of the same field restores it, a row that leaves takes it, navigation clears it. A row's control activates without selecting, and its body selects without activating; a selection's verbs ride the same zone. | smoke:a row's verbs live in the frame's row zone, one beneath another, and the frame opens as they arrive
| a-readout-that-acts-says-so | A readout that can be pressed wears the press as a capsule, says what the press does, and acts on the listing the operator can see: the header's ERRORED figure opens the runs roster filtered to the feeds it counted, and never acts on a pane that is not on stage. | smoke:the ERRORED figure opens the roster filtered to errored feeds
| a-control-lives-where-it-acts | A control sits with the thing it acts on: the drawer acts on the pane, the mode frame on what the field holds, a row's track on that row. A verb that acts on a row never rides the pane's drawer, and a control that cannot act on what the field holds stands down rather than misleading. | smoke:a browser's drawer has two groups
| an-absent-value-is-a-question | A value the command needs and was not given is asked for, never guessed and never answered with a usage line — a flag given no value and a missing required operand are the same sentence. The ask says where to look and, only when it has one worth offering, what to offer; a verb whose only candidate is the operand it already has offers nothing. Abandoning it does nothing and says so. | test:fs-destination
| an-ask-borrows-an-instrument | A question for a value that lives in a navigable space opens the pane that already shows that space, beside the pane that asked, closing when the errand ends either way. Its own controls ride a bar of its own — question, answer, one committing verb reading what it will do — never a dialog, and never the mode frame, which answers to the field. The browsing that answers a question runs beside the command that asked it, never behind it. | smoke:a location ask opens a browser beside the asker
| a-question-is-answered-where-the-session-speaks | A question the SESSION puts to a surface is stated in the console, answered there, and kept in the scrollback — and a closed console exposes itself to carry one, since a question nobody can see is a question nobody can answer (a question the SURFACE puts stands on its own pane instead: `a-question-stands-where-it-was-asked`) — never in a box, and never silently refused. A secret is masked and never enters the transcript; a yes/no is two capsules; a question outranks the command line while it is open; and Esc abandons, which the asking command is told about rather than left waiting on. | smoke:the session can put a question to this surface
| a-server-is-named-never-swept | Choosing which PACS to ask is the FILTER gesture, not a dropdown: a cell that reads its own state unfolds a strip of segments, lit when included, retracting to the field or to Esc. No control offers ALL — a fan-out across servers of unknown liveness is thousands of questions into the void — so a fan-out is always something the operator named. One chosen is a context and moves the session; several is a query and moves nothing. | smoke:the strip offers no ALL
| a-set-says-what-became-of-every-member | A listing that answers for a SET names every member of it and what became of each: found, none, and could-not-ask are three answers, not two. A miss reads as a zero and a failure never does — a zero says the PACS answered, which is the one thing a timeout did not do — and the counts a surface reads out are derived from the rows, so a summary cannot disagree with what is on screen. | smoke:every patient asked gets a row, misses included
| progress-sums-its-levels | Progress on a row that stands over other rows is the sum of theirs, never their average, and a row with nothing scheduled still carries a dim track — an absent bar reads as "no such thing" where a dim track reads as "nothing has happened yet". | smoke:a study heads its series with caps and a summed track, and the series verbs live in the frame
| pane-is-the-subscription | A pane showing a running feed holds a watch on it and releases it on leaving; the bar reports LIVE / SETTLED / STALE; the kernel never polls CUBE on its own. (The calypso daemon reading its own code on local disk once a minute, to know whether it is stale, is not CUBE traffic and does not break this.) | smoke:entering a feed reports its liveness
| a-daemon-is-restarted-by-its-own-operator | Nobody restarts a user's calypso daemon from under them. Behind a door, the operator restarts their own: the RESTART pill beside LOG OUT, or the cure in the out-of-date notice. Both ask first, in the notice's spot, naming what a restart would cut off right now. The door does it and keeps the login, and every other surface is told why it dropped. | smoke:behind a door the RESTART pill asks first
| a-component-lands-with-its-reference | A component of the surface — a thing a pane declares rather than builds — lands with its section in `components.adoc` in the same pull request: purpose, anatomy (drawn and labelled, and photographed in each state once on stage), the declaration, its states, the laws it carries, its stylesheet hooks, and what not to do with it; and every slice that changes the component amends the section. Doctrine says what the surface is; the reference says how a pane gets one, and a component without a reference is a private API wearing a public name. | lint:a-component-lands-with-its-reference
| a-fact-has-one-source | A fact the surface needs in many places is written once and read everywhere: the four sides and all they imply (`app/sides.ts`), the pane bar's states and their one writer (`features/roster/bar.ts`), the primaries and the pane kinds as a union (`app/panes.ts`), the chords (`DRAWER_CHORDS`). The lint holds every other file to those sources; a re-spelling fails CI. | lint:a-fact-has-one-source
| a-part-stays-a-part | No function runs past 150 lines: about what one tall screen shows whole. A module factory (`*_wire`, `*_build`) is a boundary, measured by the named functions inside it. A function still past the ceiling is named in the lint with its reason and the length it stood at when the ceiling arrived: it may shrink, never grow, and a new one fails CI. A file past 1,200 lines is named as a warning, never a failure. | lint:a-part-stays-a-part
| a-question-is-answered-where-the-hand-is | A question the surface asks stands on the pane that asked it and on the console's input line at once; either answers it, the first answer wins, and the other place closes. The console's transcript records every answer (a secret masked). A line typed at the prompt while a question stands is the question's answer, never a command. | smoke:a pane question stands on the console too
| keys-are-documented-from-one-table | The chords have one source, `DRAWER_CHORDS` in the console language; everything that shows them reads it — the host (the keys, the capsule titles), the console (`argus keys`, `help keys`), the HELP pane (its rows, and the dashboard's HELP tile) and the written reference `docs/keys.adoc`, which a script generates and the lint holds to the table. Nothing about a key is written by hand twice. | lint:keys-are-documented
| a-drawer-verb-has-a-chord | With a drawer open (the prefix pressed), one key presses one of its capsules; the arrows focus the pane in that direction. A chord is a key on a capsule, never a verb of its own: the table is `DRAWER_CHORDS`, one source, read by the host for the keys and the titles and by the console for `argus keys`. A key typed into a line is never a chord. | smoke:a chord presses a drawer verb
| a-frame-is-reachable-by-keys | Whatever a pane's mode frame offers, the keys reach. Over a listing, with nothing else holding the keys, ↑↓ (Home, End) move a row cursor, Enter indicates the row so its verbs ride the frame, and Enter again goes, as the mouse's second press does: through the row's control cell where it declares one (a study folding open its series). → or Ctrl-B r takes the keys into the frame, its first verb focused as soon as the frame shows; Tab or ↑↓ walk the verbs, Enter presses, ← or Esc leaves. Esc in a pane's own line (the PACS form) gives the keys back to the stage, and a focus move by the prefix (an arrow, o, ;) takes them out of the console. Ctrl-B & closes every pane on the stage (`pane close all`). | smoke keys-reach-the-frame; unit keysListing.test
| a-pane-can-be-moved | A pane on stage can be moved to a side after it was split there. The drawer's SPLIT and MOVE pills say what the four direction capsules do: under SPLIT (lit at rest, sticky) a direction opens a new pane; under MOVE (one press, then the drawer falls back to SPLIT) a direction moves this pane — detached, re-split onto the nearest block on that side (its former sibling when there is none), even — so every outcome is a tree a split could have made. A side it cannot go dims before the press; the mover keeps focus; its bar reads MOVED <SIDE> for a moment and the console carries nothing from the capsule; `pane move <side>` is the same verb typed, refused by name. | smoke:a pane moves where the drawer's MOVE sends it
| a-red-is-never-pre-existing | The full smoke runs every night against a fresh private daemon as the e2e identity (`scripts/smoke-nightly.sh`, the `mise-smoke.timer` user unit; `make smoke-report` reads the ledger), at the desk width, and the listing stages again at a tablet width and under a finger. A red is a defect or a stale test and is read the next day; it is never carried as "pre-existing" — four were, for days, in 2026-10 (#899), and a red nobody reads is a test nobody has. | the ledger at ~/.local/state/mise/smoke/ledger.txt
| a-verification-sees-two-widths-and-a-finger | A live verification of a surface change is made at the desk width and at 1024 px, and under a coarse pointer, with real browser input (CDP mouse and touch, never synthetic DOM events alone); a session-affecting change is verified with a second identity. One daemon, one identity, one width and a mouse missed the squeezed PROGRESS column, the unfindable grips and the two-identity confusion in a single day. | smoke-nightly tablet and finger runs; SMOKE_WIDTH/SMOKE_TOUCH in the driver
| a-scenario-runs-a-line-to-its-prompt | A smoke scenario that types a line at the console waits for that line's prompt before the next step, never a fixed sleep: the console declares `data-busy` on its element from a submit until the prompt returns, and the harness's `console_run` types only on an idle console and returns only when it is idle again. Fixed sleeps raced CUBE's pace: the same three-scenario run, started three times at once, gave a crash, a red and a green (2026-10-06, #919), and the nightly's "question in the wrong directory" was a `cd` that had not landed. A scenario also leaves the console idle, and the hygiene check says when one does not. | smoke stage_leaks (busy); console_run in the harness prelude; terminal.ts busy_set
| a-feed-has-one-view | A feed is one view, whichever door opens it: the RUNS roster opens it at once, the universe flies into it, and both draw it with one builder (`scene/feedGraph.ts`), frame it with one set of verbs (`features/feed/view.ts`: arrangement, projection, draw, pulse, metric, census, gravity, hue, and the feed's note, tags and name), and stand it in the modes this device last chose, whichever door turned them. A feed opens as its tree, root on top, unless the device chose a molecule. The universe keeps only what is about the whole space (its arrangements, replay, saved positions). Before, the descent drew the same `feed.dag` with its own builder as a free-form burst while RUNS drew a top-down tree with other pills — the operator: "the runs and universe rendering should both come from the same source of truth and same rendering expectation". | smoke feed-one-view (one feed through both doors: tree root on top, frame words, facts, a mode turned through one door standing through the other; orrery publishes `data-roots-top` on the canvas); unit feedGraph.test, feedView.test, feedFacts.test
| a-secret-is-never-on-the-command-line | A password or a token is never typed on a command line nor written into a script: it is in `ps` for every user of a shared host and in the file forever. A secret lives in the user's own file at mode 0600 (`~/.config/chell/doors/<door-host>.json`, written by `chell auth login`), or in an environment variable a runner injects (`CHELL_DOOR_TOKEN`), and a file that is loose (not the user's, or readable by others) is refused by name, never used, and never a reason to fall back to a prompt a script would hang on. The nightly seeds its daemon's token from a 0600 config and pipes a minted token on stdin for the same reason. | chell auth (doorFile.test: loose file refused); smoke-nightly door step; porter --mint prints once
| a-token-dies-on-its-day | A door token has a fixed horizon (`PORTER_TOKEN_DAYS`, 30) from its minting and no sliding refresh: a leaked key in steady use still ends, and every script is re-blessed by a human on the day. From seven days out every call says so on stderr; at expiry the door refuses by name and chell prints the words to run, never a prompt. A token is revocable by name (`porter --revoke`, `chell auth logout`), a running door notices a store changed under it, and a token never mints a token. | porter tokens.test (expired refused by name); chell door.test (the warning and the refusal); routes.test (revoked refused on its next call)
| a-login-never-lands-on-an-older-kernel | The door mounts current code. Calypso writes the versions it runs into its berth at boot; porter, at every login, compares them with what it ships and, where the daemon is behind, ends it and boots a fresh one on the way in (saved state kept) before mounting. Running sessions are left alone — the stale warnings in ARGUS and chell cover a daemon whose code moved under someone mid-work — but nobody arrives on yesterday's kernel. | porter routes.test (a login onto an older daemon restarts it); scripts/smoke-upgrade.sh nightly (a published N−1 daemon, this checkout's porter: one restart, then attach; catches the #913 versions drop)
| the-expanse-is-never-sized-by-hand | A listing's columns may be sized by hand, the way a file manager's are: a drag on a grip moves the boundary under it with the pointer, and the two columns it separates trade the width, each within its declared floor; a boundary never moves against the hand, and stops where a side has nothing left to give. A double-press gives both columns back, the frame's COLUMNS block gives all back, and what was sized is the device's memory (`localStorage`), since a tablet and a desk want different widths. The one `1fr` expanse is never given a width: beside a boundary it gives or takes the difference while it has room to stretch, so the alignment the expanse rule bought — bars at one x across levels — survives any sizing; pinned at its minimum (a row wider than the pane) it is moved like a fixed column, and only that minimum changes. On a row already wider than its pane (a tablet) the neighbours are all at their minimums and have nothing to trade, so the column left of the boundary simply grows and the row grows with it, as Finder's and Explorer's list views do; shrinking still stops at the column's floor. (The first build sized the column left of the grip and let the expanse absorb: with the expanse on the left, as NAME is on a files listing, a column grew away from the hand and the NAME boundary did nothing — the operator, 2026-10-06: "completely and totally counterintuitive".) The block reads `COLUMNS · SIZED` while anything is sized (a-readout-that-acts-says-so). | smoke listing-columns; unit columns.test.ts
| a-row-never-runs-under-the-rail | A listing's row ends inside its field: when the pane is too narrow for every column, the columns that declare a rank give way lowest first until the rest fit, and the bar says how many stood aside (`n COLUMNS HIDDEN`); nothing is clipped without a word, and the frame's corner never covers a cell. A wider pane brings them back. | argus `listing` (`listingState_compose`), live measurement at 1333×833×1.5
| a-readout-names-the-release-it-reads | Every surface of the release notes says which release it reads: the NOTES pane's bar names the daemon's installed versions, the dashboard's WHAT'S NEW block reads AFTER RESTART when the daemon's disk has moved, and the stale-page notice's one line of news is the page's own bundle's — the page being the one ahead. A terminal attached to a stale daemon says so in the same words, with its own newest headline. | smoke notes, stale-page; chell remoteClient.test
| a-field-says-it-holds-more | Every scrolling field hides the browser's bar and wears one chip at its foot — `▼ N MORE` while content lies below, `▲ TOP` at the end — through the one helper, `more_wire`. A container whose child is the wired field does not scroll itself. The console's scrollback keeps the terminal's idiom, by name. A save is the line the operator could have typed, `touch --withContents='<text>' '<path>'`, echoed in the console and run through the kernel, so the transcript holds every save and a feed's note is written through its projection like any file; the pane reaches no byte route. The kernel's `edit` opens the pane and is answered at once that it opened. A second `edit` of the same file finds its pane and leaves unsaved changes standing. | lint:an-editors-save-is-a-line
| a-holder-is-a-mark-and-a-line | Who can read a feed besides its owner — each user, each group, and PUBLIC when it is — are marks on its indicated row's readout, each with a × that asks on the pane ("Stop sharing feed_N with …?") before it withdraws, by `setfacl -x u:`/`g:`, or `setfacl -m o::-` for PUBLIC. SHARE asks a choose question: the CUBE groups the operator belongs to and EVERYONE as pills, the ones that already hold dimmed, a field for a user; each pick runs `setfacl -m`, and the question comes back until DONE. Every change is a visible line: TAG's question (the user's tags as pills, the worn ones dimmed, a field for a new one, asked again until DONE) runs `setfattr -n tag`, after `mkdir /proc/tags/<name>` for a new tag, and × runs `setfattr -x`. | tags-follow-the-image | A tags pane is slaved, never detached: it lists the elements of the instance the image pane in its link group has on screen, re-asking the kernel (`dcm tags`) on every slice the image pane regards, and it never lists a file on its own account except the one a `dcm tags` claim handed it. | series-loads-by-consent | The first slice costs one file and shows at once. Over the guard, the stack and every volume layout (MPR, 3D) wait: the bar reads `SERIES <size> · LOAD FOR <STACK\|MPR\|3D>`, a LOAD block appears on the frame, and nothing more is fetched until LOAD, `image load`, or `image --force <path>` says so. The probe is a readout, not a mark: it says what is under the pointer while the pointer is there and leaves nothing on the image. | smoke:the field keeps its shape when the frame opens beside it
| a-press-answers-at-once | A click that opens an image is answered before the read is: `image_open` puts the pane on stage at the click, the field says what is being opened (`OPENING <name>`) and the bar says the same, before the series read returns. A typed `image <path>` is the kernel command instead, and the surface opens the pane from the intent it returns; either way the press yields a pane, and what could not open is named where it would have appeared — never only in the console, the pane never blank. | smoke:image <path> is a kernel command whose intent opens the pane
| a-program-may-take-the-screen | A program may take the whole screen and must give it back: `trek` typed at the transcript console opens the bridge over ARGUS, the page hidden behind it under one body attribute, and ARGUS returns whatever ends the game: a win, a loss, LEAVE BRIDGE (Esc asks first), or a run that threw. | a-bridge-answers-the-prompt-in-front-of-it | The bridge never predicts the engine's dialogue: each control answers the question the engine has open, named from its prompt, and a pill whose question is not open presses nothing. | a-frame-replays-a-printed-line | Nothing on the bridge moves without an engine line or a change of state behind it, and the frame is the clock: the lines replay in order, each with its cue, the log scrolling with the picture. | a-surface-says-how-to-reach-it | A surface says how to reach the session it is on, from its own address and without asking the session anything: `attach` reads the location the browser arrived by and prints the terminal's line and the browser's URL for the same session. A bearer token is masked in the readout and revealed only when the operator asks for it by name, so a shared screen or a pasted scrollback carries no credential; an address on loopback says that it is, because a second machine cannot reach it. | smoke:the masked form never carries the token, and --reveal does

It is exposed as the drawer is, arrives with the press and leaves with its answer, and the console records the exchange so the scrollback keeps the whole session. A question the SESSION puts stays in the console, which exposes itself to carry it: a question in a closed console is a press that reads as dead and a surface waiting on an answer nobody can give. | smoke:a question the surface asks stands on the pane that asked, not in a closed console
| a-component-names-no-theme | A component of the surface and its reference speak the component's own vocabulary — caps, strip, frame, field, track, expanse, level, group — and name no theme. | a-share-is-seen-on-the-next-look | A feed shared to this identity is news until it is seen: it leads RUNS marked NEW with who shared it, across logins, until opened or cleared. A look that names a feed the roster lacks — `proc feeds <id>`, `ls /proc/jobs/feed_N`, a RUNS filter that is an id — asks CUBE for it live and either adopts it or says why there is none ("not shared with you, or shared to a group you are not in"); a miss is never silent. The look can be asked for: `proc feeds --fresh`, the roster's REFRESH, walk the index now; `proc jump <n>` and RUNS's JUMP TO go to a feed or node by number, asking which when a number is both; and a walk is never silent either — the bar reads `CHECKING CUBE FOR THE FEED LIST…` while one runs and `FEED LIST CURRENT · n NEW` when it lands. | salsa `procProviderFull` (moved total walks whole; `ls` adopts), brasa `proc-builtin` (`proc feeds <id>` asks; `--fresh` forces the walk), argus `listing` (`filteredEmpty`, `refresh`), smoke runs-filter-asks, runs-refresh

A desktop records a graph as those lines — every mode stated, never a toggle, and where it stands last, by `/proc` name so no number is ever asked about — so the script replays the same on any device and for any operator. A shared view starts from the baseline every session starts at, `desktop clear` (the operator's screen kept first as a PANES card, then every pane closed and the dashboard on stage), which also numbers the panes after it as a replay does, so the same lines work loaded, typed or pasted; SHARE VIEW (and `desktop share`) shows that script and copies it, saying COPIED or SELECT + COPY, and COPY LINK puts it in a link (`#view=`, readable). A files pane records the folder it shows (`file %n open <path>`). | argus `compositions/chris/lang.ts` (`dag_run`), `features/dag/panel.ts` (`dagView_lines`, `view_lines`, `target_jumpTo`), `app/consoleHost.ts` (`desktop_serialize`, `dag_jump`), `app/shareView.ts` (`viewScript_compose`, `shareSheet_open`), `console/argusLang.ts` (`desktop clear|share`, `viewScript_run`, `viewLine_refusal`), `features/feed/view.ts` (`feedModes_hold`)
